### Airplane

- **Free plan ('Start using Airplane for free')** (high) — Final form (2023-2024, last archived official pricing page 2024-03-10): Free $0/user/month with unlimited users, unlimited objects, 2 environments, 3 days of activity history. Limits evolved: 2021 early access was $0/mo unlimited tasks+users; Mar 2022 free tier was up to 5 users + up to 5 runbooks/tasks + 3-day activity log; Sep 2022 was up to 5 users + up to 50 objects; by Sep 2023 it was unlimited users/objects + 2 environments + 3-day history (the widely-cited '5 internal tools, unlimited users' line is an intermediate 2022-era limit, not the final state). 'Objects' = internal apps (UI/API serverless functions).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone (personal or company). No .edu email, no startup stage/funding requirement. No credit card requirement documented on any archived page; sign-up was GitHub-login based ('Sign Up' button), Free tier had a 'Get started' CTA.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at airplane.dev with GitHub, choose the Free plan ($0) - no form, no credits to redeem. Paid Team ($10/user/mo) and Business ($40/user/mo) tiers were marked 'Try for free'.
  - *Expiry:* EXPIRED - service sunset 2024-03-01 (acquisition by Airtable); new signups closed 2024-01-03. No offer exists as of 2026-10-22.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: the free plan was genuinely perpetual while live, but the whole platform was sunset so nothing persists. Data: 3-day activity-history cap on Free; self-hosted agents, group permissions and env/embed features were paid-only. Self-hosted agents were the only data-egress mitigation; no public egress/egress-fee schedule on Free. The live URL now redirects in a 301 loop on a Squarespace parking backend - any link to airplane.dev/pricing in old articles is dead.
  - *Source:* https://www.airplane.dev/pricing(dead;archivedcopiesreadtoday:https://web.archive.org/web/20240310140454/https://www.airplane.dev/pricing,https://web.archive.org/web/20230926132128/https://www.airplane.dev/pricing,https://web.archive.org/web/20220325175750/https://www.airplane.dev/pricing)
- **Airplane for Startups - up to $10,000 in credits** (high) — Program ('Scalable tools for fast-moving teams'): eligible startups could redeem up to $10,000 in Airplane credits, valid for a year. Page was live from at least 2023-03 through its last 200 capture 2024-02-29, and already returning 404 by 2024-05-16. Intro blog post 'introducing-airplane-for-startups' appeared 2024-01-04.
  - *Eligibility:* Three stated requirements: (1) fewer than 50 employees, (2) Series A or earlier (stage cap - no explicit dollar funding figure), (3) not a current paying customer. Work email required on the form (no .edu angle, it was a startup program); no region limits stated.
  - *How to claim:* Fill out the form on airplane.dev/startups ('Redeem your credits'): name, work email, company name, and an optional 'Anything specific you want to use Airplane for?' field; approval was manual. No credit card required. Program was dead once Airplane itself shut down.
  - *Expiry:* EXPIRED - program ended with platform sunset 2024-03-01; /startups 404 by 2024-05-16. $10,000 of credits for a year is unredeemable and the platform it applied to no longer exists.
  - *Gotchas:* Credits were Airplane-platform-only (valueless post-sunset). The 'up to $10,000' was a cap, not a guaranteed amount. 'Series A or earlier' is the only funding/stage gate. No student/education variant of this program ever existed.
  - *Source:* https://www.airplane.dev/startups(dead;archivedcopiesreadtoday:https://web.archive.org/web/20240229194024/https://www.airplane.dev/startups;FAQ/announcement:https://web.archive.org/web/20240104062134/https://www.airplane.dev/blog/introducing-airplane-for-startups)
- **Free trials on paid plans (Team 'Try for free', Business 'Try for free')** (medium) — Team at $10/user/mo and Business at $40/user/mo carried 'Try for free' CTAs on the 2023-2024 pricing page; trial duration and post-trial billing terms were not published on the page. (Enterprise was custom, 'Book demo'.)
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone signing up; no startup/stage restrictions; no .edu requirement. No credit-card requirement documented on the archived page (sign-up via GitHub login).
  - *How to claim:* Click 'Try for free' on the Team or Business tier at airplane.dev/pricing.
  - *Expiry:* EXPIRED - all signups closed 2024-01-03; platform sunset 2024-03-01.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial terms (duration, CC capture) were not published and cannot be verified; treat as non-existent now.
  - *Source:* https://www.airplane.dev/pricing(dead;archivedcopyreadtoday:https://web.archive.org/web/20240310140454/https://www.airplane.dev/pricing)
- **2025–26 change:** FULLY SUNSET - nothing live as of 2026-10-22. Timeline (verified today via DNS, live probing and Wayback): (1) 2024-01-03: Airtable announces acquisition of Airplane in official blog 'Airtable acquires Airplane' (archived: https://web.archive.org/web/20240310140454/https://www.airplane.dev/blog/airtable); same post states 'we will unfortunately be sunsetting the Airplane platform on March 1' and that new signups had already closed. (2) 2024-03-01: platform sunset date. (3) 2024-05-16: /startups already returning 404. (4) No 200-status HTML captures of any airplane.dev page (incl. subdomains) exist after mid-March 2024; all 2025-2026 captures are 301s. (5) As of 2026-10-22 the domain itself is lapsed/parked: authoritative Googledomains NS shows apex A-record 198.49.23.145 (NSI-style parking IP) and NXDOMAIN for www.airplane.dev; www paths serve self-referential 301 loops behind a 'Server: Squarespace' header, apex 301s to www - a broken-redirect parking loop, so even the parked page won't render. GitHub orgs 'rethink-dev' and 'getairplane' both 404. The team was absorbed into Airtable; Airtable did not continue Airplane as a standalone product on that domain. No education/student offer existed at any point: no /education, /students, or /student-programs pages in the archive and zero 'student'/'.edu' mentions on archived pricing/startup pages. Note the sunsetting happened in 2024, not 2025-2026; during 2025-2026 the domain simply went from dead to parked - no offer changes. All claims above therefore rely on archived copies of the official pages (read today), not on live ones, since the live pages no longer exist.
- **Critic correction** (Airplane): Discontinued. Airtable acquired Airplane in January 2024, closed signups and shut the platform down on March 1, 2024. The free plan no longer exists. Remove this entry.

### CircleCI

- **Free plan (Cloud)** (high) — Permanent free tier on CircleCI Cloud: 30,000 credits/month (= up to 6,000 build minutes on small Docker at 5 credits/min; ~3,000 min on medium Docker at 10 credits/min). Up to 5 active users/month; 30x concurrency (Docker, Linux VM, Arm, Windows VM) but only 1 macOS concurrency; 5 concurrent self-hosted runner tasks. Includes 2 GB storage + 1 GB network transfer; excess network/storage draws from your monthly credits at ~420 credits/GB. 1,000 Smarter Testing test atoms/month free. Free plan has no GPU classes and no X-large/large+ resource classes (Docker capped at Large; macOS M4 Pro Large and all GPU classes are paid-only).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone; no .edu email, no application, no credit card required (page states 'No credit card required'). New signups land on Free plan. From 2025-12-01 each unregistered commit author counts individually toward the 5 active-user limit (previously counted as one).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up at circleci.com/signup (GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket/Cursor Origin), 2) connect a repo and add project, 3) builds run automatically on the Free plan — no plan selection or payment step needed.
  - *Expiry:* Recurring monthly; the 30,000 free credits expire at end of each billing month with no rollover. No sunset date. When credits hit 0, personal builds are blocked until the next month's refill.
  - *Gotchas:* Small-Docker framing makes it look like 6,000 min but on medium Docker you get ~3,000 min; on macOS M4 Pro (200 credits/min) the 30k pool lasts only ~150 min. 5-user cap bites if many contributors push to private repos. Credits are non-refundable; unused paid credits forfeit on cancellation (Free credits simply don't roll over).
  - *Source:* https://circleci.com/pricing/
- **Open source build pool (free OSS program)** (high) — Free-plan orgs building public repos get up to 400,000 credits/month for Linux, Arm, and Docker OSS builds (= up to 80,000 build minutes on small Docker) on top of the 30,000-credit personal pool, plus 30,000 credits/month for macOS and Windows OSS builds. Includes enterprise-grade features (Docker layer caching, SSH debugging, flaky-test detection, self-hosted runners). OSS builds keep running even when your personal 30k pool is exhausted, and unregistered OSS contributors (e.g., PR authors) do not count toward your seat limit.
  - *Eligibility:* Free-plan organizations whose repository is public (GitHub, GitLab, or Bitbucket) and meets open-source licensing; OSS must be enabled in the project settings. No application, approval, or credit card; no .edu required; not region-limited. Docs note the OSS credit availability/limits are not visible in the web app.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create the account on Free plan, 2) keep the repo public on GitHub/GitLab/Bitbucket, 3) enable open source in project settings (per CircleCI staff guidance in the Dec-2025 billing change thread) — the extra pool then applies automatically, no form needed.
  - *Expiry:* Recurring monthly (400k refreshes each month), no rollover, no end date. The official open-source page states 30,000 credits for macOS/Windows, but a May 2026 support article states 25,000 credits for macOS — treat the macOS/Windows sub-pool as 25k–30k pending CircleCI confirmation.
  - *Gotchas:* OSS credit pool is invisible in the UI; if you're blocked from building you must open a support ticket. Windows/macOS OSS capacity is tiny vs Linux/Arm/Docker. The official macOS number is inconsistent across CircleCI's own pages (30k on pricing/open-source vs 25k in support docs), so plan around the lower figure.
  - *Source:* https://circleci.com/open-source/
- **Startup Program (CircleCI for Startups)** (high) — Up to $20,000 of free compute credits (at the $0.0006/credit rate, roughly 33M credits) usable on Docker, Linux, Arm, macOS, and Windows, spread over up to 24 months free, plus free Starter Support normally valued at $249/month, plus guided onboarding/migration support. Exact credit amount and program length vary by startup stage and location; it ends when the credits are exhausted or the promotional window closes, whichever comes first.
  - *Eligibility:* All must hold: (1) new to CircleCI or on the Free plan (no current paid customers), (2) Pre-Seed through Series A (Series B+ excluded), (3) founded within the last 5 years, (4) part of a recognized accelerator or VC, or otherwise proof of at least $500K USD raised (minimum threshold; no revenue cap or headcount cap stated). No .edu; no explicit region exclusion, but amounts/duration vary by location. No credit-card requirement is mentioned on the page.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Fill out the application form on circleci.com/startup-program/ (HubSpot form), 2) or email startups@circleci.com (accelerators/VCs can enroll portfolio companies there), 3) CircleCI decides your tier on application; a representative contacts you as credits run low; after the window you move to standard pricing with volume discounts, config/history carry over with zero disruption.
  - *Expiry:* Promotional period: up to 24 months or until credits are fully consumed, whichever comes first. Program itself has no stated sunset date. Unused credits are program-scoped, not refundable/cashable.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Up to' figures — the actual tier (credit count and months) is assigned by CircleCI, not chosen by you, and varies by location. If you burn the pool early you transition to paid; the 'more credits than standard Performance' pitch still leaves you on a billable plan afterward. Not available to companies already paying for CircleCI or founded 5+ years ago.
  - *Source:* https://circleci.com/startup-program/
- **Education license / student offer — none active (Academy sunsets)** (medium) — CircleCI has no student-specific free offer or .edu-based license: students simply use the standard Free plan (30,000 credits/month) with a personal account. The former free education platform, CircleCI Academy (free courses and the Associate Developer certificate, once accessible with code public-2020), is no longer available — CircleCI's support 'Educational resources' collection states 'CircleCI Academy is no longer available' and offers all 17 former courses/certificates only as downloadable SCORM files (course + exam links via Google Drive) that you can import into your own LMS or run locally. Third-party 2026 discount roundups (CostBench, Erkan Saka) confirm no CircleCI education terms are published.
  - *Eligibility:* Formerly: anyone (public-2020 access code) for Academy courses. Now: course/exam downloads are public; no .edu email or enrollment proof needed for anything. There is no student pricing, discount, or license program to apply for.
  - *How to claim:* Nothing to claim for a student license. For the learning content: go to the 'Educational resources' collection on support.circleci.com and download the SCORM courses; for actual CI compute, just sign up for the Free plan or the OSS pool with a public repo.
  - *Expiry:* CircleCI Academy is already sunset (confirmed as of 2026-10-22; no end date published). The SCORM downloads remain available. Free plan / OSS pool have no end dates.
  - *Gotchas:* Treat any cached third-party pages advertising 'CircleCI for students' as stale. A support article (May 2026) still says macOS OSS gets 25,000 credits vs the pricing page's 30,000 — documentation is slightly out of sync. Certificate no longer issued, only self-study downloads.
  - *Source:* https://support.circleci.com/en/collections/19634789-educational-resources
- **2025–26 change:** 2025–2026 changes and absences: (1) No free trial credits on paid plans currently exist — the pricing page (read 2026-10-22) lists no trial duration, trial credit pool, or CC-for-trial offer, and an independent 2026-10-14 tracker (PulseSignal) records "Free trial: None listed"; the trial-circleci model has been superseded by the permanent 30,000-credit Free plan. (2) CircleCI Academy (free courses/certificate, formerly the closest thing to an education program) has been sunset — the support collection now says "CircleCI Academy is no longer available"; only SCORM downloads of the 17 former courses remain; no end date was published, but it was already gone by 2026-10-22. (3) Effective 2025-12-01, CircleCI changed unregistered-user billing: every unregistered user now counts individually toward the monthly active-user total (previously all unregistered users counted as one); the Free plan keeps 5 included active users at the same price, and OSS-enabled projects are exempt (unregistered OSS PR authors don't count as seats). (4) The Free plan's 30,000-credit monthly allowance has been stable through 2025–2026 (no observed change; PriceVersion shows $0/30,000 credits recorded as current from 2026-10-04); the 400k OSS Linux pool and 30k macOS/Windows OSS pool are also still live per the official open-source page, though the support doc (May 2026) still lists macOS OSS at 25,000 credits — a documentation inconsistency worth confirming with CircleCI support. (5) Related non-offer sunsets: Bitbucket Data Center support retired 2026-10-01 and Self-Hosted Runner Bitbucket Cloud retires 2026-11-12 (VCS deprecations, not pricing); Xcode image retention is rolling off older versions each month.

### Cursor

- **Free plan ($0/mo) — standing free tier, no trial** (high) — Official pricing page lists a Free plan at $0/month with 'Limited AI usage': 'Limited agent requests' and 'Limited Chat requests' (no numeric caps published; the docs page marks free included-usage as simply 'Limited'). Free tier includes 'Post, reply, and follow'; Grok Chat/Imagine/Build are 'Limited'; third-party models, Grok API, cloud agents, code hosting, AI code reviews are not available ('—') on Free. Usage allowance refreshes weekly ('one heavy day never wipes out your whole month'); when exhausted, users 'can keep going with top-offs, or wait for your allowance to refresh'; remaining usage visible in settings. No free trial or starter credits are advertised anywhere on the current pricing page.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone can sign up; no .edu email, no proof of affiliation, and no credit card required for the free tier (page states only that self-serve paid plans support major credit/debit cards). No region limits stated on the page. Prices exclude tax (VAT).
  - *How to claim:* Fetched https://cursor.com/pricing and https://cursor.com/docs/models-and-pricing today (2026-10-22): Free plan $0/mo, limited agent/chat requests, weekly refresh, top-ups optional, no trial or CC requirement stated; paid tiers Plus $8 / Premium $30 / Super $100 / Ultra $200 per month.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite free tier; weekly usage refresh; no expiry date, no trial clock.
  - *Gotchas:* Exact request limits are not published on the page (only 'Limited') — plan accordingly. Third-party model usage is billed separately at API list price even on paid tiers; 'Plus ($8/mo) is Grok-models-only', so cheapest paid tier is deliberately restricted. There is no advertised free-trial of paid plans, so free users get less than historical promo/credit offers. Cancellation policy: cancel at least 24h before renewal to avoid charges.
  - *Source:* https://cursor.com/pricing(corroboratedbyhttps://cursor.com/docs/models-and-pricing)
- **Campus Leads program — free 6 months of Cursor Ultra for student community leaders** (high) — Cursor's 'Campus Leads' program (run twice yearly, fall and spring) accepts current university students who commit ~5 hours/week to building the campus community (workshops, hackathons, meetups, partnering with clubs, product feedback). Benefits: all-expenses-paid trip to Cursor's San Francisco office for kick-off, custom swag, travel reimbursement to one hackathon from a verified list, early access to new features, invitation to a private Slack with the Cursor team, funding/resources to host on-campus events, and 'Access to 6 months of Cursor Ultra' — the top tier, worth $200/mo per the pricing page (~$1,200 value, plus travel). Eligibility criteria: current university student, completed application, accepted Code of Conduct, 5h/week commitment.
  - *Eligibility:* Must be a current university student (no .edu stated as a check method, but student status required). Selective admission — application + Code of Conduct required. No funding-cap or startup-age criteria (this is a student/community program, not a startup program). No credit card mentioned for acceptance.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to cursor.com/campus-leads. 2) Fill out the interest Typeform (hosted by Anysphere) to be notified when applications reopen. 3) Apply when the cycle opens; accept the Cursor Community Code of Conduct (linked Notion). 4) Direct questions to students@cursor.com. Note the page says 'Applications reopen in January' — i.e., the spring intake window.
  - *Expiry:* Program runs on two cycles per year (fall/spring); the Ultra access lasts 6 months per cycle. Applications reopen in January for the spring cycle; fall cycle via the interest form.
  - *Gotchas:* Only accepted Campus Leads get the free Ultra — it is not a blanket student license; general students still use the free 'Limited' tier. Perks are program-bound (renewal means staying an active lead / re-entering the next cycle). The 6-month Ultra benefit plus travel is the entirety of the free-value offer; no monetary refund or seat sharing is mentioned.
  - *Source:* https://cursor.com/campus-leads
- **Student discounts/promotions (on-campus & online events, fall cycle) — amounts not fixed** (medium) — The dedicated students page (cursor.com/students) confirms 'Anyone can get started with Cursor for free' and, in its FAQ, says: 'To upgrade your access, look out for promotions at our on-campus and online events starting this fall.' A linked Typeform sign-up advertises 'early access to upcoming events, exclusive discounts, and beginner guides.' No dollar amount, percentage, tier, seat count, code or credit amount is published on the page; the concrete student benefit available today is the Campus Leads Ultra benefit above.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone who signs up for the free tier (no .edu required for free start). Event-based student promotions target on-campus/online participants (students/educators at institutions); no explicit .edu verification stated. No credit-card requirement stated for the free start.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign up for Cursor free (no card). 2) Subscribe to the Typeform newsletter on cursor.com/students for event early access and discount offers. 3) Attend on-campus/online events (fall cycle) to receive promotions. 4) Optionally apply to Campus Leads for the 6-month Ultra benefit.
  - *Expiry:* Tied to the fall/spring event calendar ('starting this fall'); promotions are event-specific with no published expiry or renewal terms; the newsletter early access persists.
  - *Gotchas:* No exact discount figures, dates or limits are published — do not treat 'exclusive discounts' as a known amount. The event-based promotion is not a standing license; it may differ from the Campus Leads benefit. Verify current event terms at each event, since pages say 'look out for' rather than commit to a price.
  - *Source:* https://cursor.com/students
- **2025–26 change:** No startup program found: cursor.com/startups returned only a page title with no program, credits or eligibility content, and neither the pricing page nor the models-and-pricing docs reference any startup offer, funding cap or startup-age criterion. No trial-credit offer is advertised: the current official pages list no free trial of paid plans and no starter credits (historical new-user trial/promo-credit offers from 2024–2025 are absent from the pricing/docs pages as of today; I could not run web searches this session to confirm sunset dates, so this is 'not listed on official pages today' rather than a verified end date). Material 2025–2026 structural changes observable on the current pages: tier lineup and pricing are now Plus $8/mo, Premium $30/mo, Super $100/mo, Ultra $200/mo (Teams $30/$100/$200 per seat; Enterprise custom), with pools expressed as multiples of Plus and a weekly refresh; the Free plan's exact request counts are unpublished ('Limited'); third-party models are billed at API list price with spending caps, and Teams/Enterprise add a $0.25/million-token 'Cursor Token Rate' on eligible third-party usage. A model promo (e.g., GPT-5.6 'Promotional pricing through November 21, 2026') appears in the docs, but it is a pricing promo, not a student/startup offer.

### Datadog

- **Datadog Free Plan (Infrastructure monitoring)** (high) — Free monitoring plan at $0: up to 5 hosts, 1-day metric retention, 5 containers per host (FAQ says '5 or 10 containers free for each host license'), unlimited user accounts, 'core collection and visualization features', 1,000+ integrations. The current pricing table does NOT show custom-metric/custom-event allotments in the Free column; its FAQ attributes '100 custom metrics/500 custom events per host' to Pro and '200/1000' to Enterprise — so the previously advertised 100-metric/500-event free allotment is no longer explicitly granted on the Free tier as displayed today. Paid reference points: Infrastructure Pro $15/host/mo (annual, 15-month retention), Enterprise $23/host/mo.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to any new Datadog account holder; no .edu email, no startup proof, no credit card stated for the free plan; no region restriction stated. Data region chosen at signup cannot later be migrated.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up at app.datadoghq.com/register (no credit card required) -> choose data region -> the 5-host Free plan applies automatically to the account; no application or referral needed.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite; no expiration or renewal. 1-day metric retention means data older than 1 day is purged; plan limits (5 hosts) are fixed.
  - *Gotchas:* 1-day retention vs 15 months on paid plans; container allotment (5 or 10 per host) is a soft cap; over 5 hosts or over 10 containers/host requires a paid plan; the 100 custom metrics/500 custom events per-host allotments appear tied to paid Pro/Enterprise in today's pricing FAQ, so verify current free-tier custom-metric behavior in-product. 'Unlimited alerts' and 'unlimited users' are not explicitly printed in the Free column on today's page (they appear under Pro/Enterprise rows).
  - *Source:* https://www.datadoghq.com/pricing/
- **Agent Observability (LLM Observability) Free Tier** (high) — Per today's pricing page: Free tier gives 'Up to 40K LLM spans / month', '15-day trace retention', 'Unlimited context & evals', 'Full feature access', positioned 'For individuals and small teams', and the FAQ states 'up to 40K LLM spans per month with 15-day retention ... no credit card required.' Paid contrast: Pro 'Starting At $160 per month' includes 100K LLM spans/month; overage 'Additional spans: $3.50 per 10K LLM spans'.
  - *Eligibility:* Any Datadog account holder; individuals and small teams; no .edu or startup proof; no credit card required; no region limit stated.
  - *How to claim:* Included with a standard Datadog account (sign up free, no CC) -> connect LLM/agent telemetry via OpenTelemetry OpenInference integrations or supported LLM SDK integrations -> spans are tracked up to 40K/month with no application.
  - *Expiry:* 40K LLM-span allowance resets monthly; 15-day rolling trace retention; no end date. Exceeding 40K spans on the free tier pushes you to the paid Pro tier pricing.
  - *Gotchas:* Renamed from LLM Observability to Agent Observability; 'full feature access' is on the free tier but the product was recently GA-transitioned, so some features may be in preview; 15-day retention is short vs 15-month metric retention on paid infra plans; span overages are not billed on free — they are the trigger to upgrade; egress/scanning costs (e.g., AI Guard at $0.80/1M tokens scanned) are separate paid line items if enabled.
  - *Source:* https://www.datadoghq.com/pricing/
- **14-day Free Trial (metrics, APM/traces, logs, Synthetics)** (high) — Official trial page: '14 days of unlimited monitoring' covering 'metrics, traces, and logs', log search/analysis, APM request tracing, and Synthetic-like monitoring of 'applications and API endpoints via simulated user requests', plus anomaly/outlier/forecast alerts. 'No credit card required' is stated on the page. 'Unlimited' during trial means no host limit for the trial window; at trial end the account drops to plan-based limits (free plan for infra).
  - *Eligibility:* Any new Datadog user; no .edu email or startup proof required; no credit card; region choice at signup is binding ('You can't migrate data between regions').
  - *How to claim:* Click any 'Start Free Trial' CTA (or datadoghq.com/trial) -> create account with no CC -> pick data region -> monitoring is live immediately for 14 days; no application, no sales call.
  - *Expiry:* 14 days from signup; not renewable as a trial (one trial per account in practice). After it ends, only the free-plan limits (e.g., 5 hosts) and the 40K LLM-span tier apply; paid products require a plan.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free confusion: 'unlimited' applies only during the 14 days; post-trial you keep the free plan, not unlimited monitoring. No post-trial behavior is stated on the page (data retention/downgrade specifics not published). Region cannot be changed later. Trial does not include all products (e.g., Cloud SIEM/Security add-ons have separate trial offers).
  - *Source:* https://www.datadoghq.com/trial/
- **Datadog for Startups (DDFS) — up to $100K credits** (high) — Official startup page: 'up to a year of free access to the full Datadog platform — up to $100k in credits', hero states 'a year of free Datadog Pro'. Coverage: complete GA product suite (Observability: infrastructure/APM/logs/data; Security: code/cloud/SIEM; Digital Experience; Software Delivery; Service Management; AI modules) with 'No watered-down tiers, no hidden limits'; preview products 'if space permits'. Extras: optional Technical Engagement Manager, documentation access, customer-success rep after several months.
  - *Eligibility:* (1) Funding stage 'Series A or earlier' (the page's cap is funding-stage based, no explicit employee/revenue figures published); (2) 'New to Datadog' — no current/prior Datadog customers and no prior DDFS credits; (3) 'Referred by one of our partners' (VCs, hyperscalers, accelerators) — applications without a partner referral are not accepted. No .edu component.
  - *How to claim:* Get a referral from a partner (VC/hyperscaler/accelerator) -> submit the application at datadoghq.com/startups describing your team -> start a Datadog trial while it is reviewed -> acceptance notified in ~3-5 business days -> activate within 30 days after acceptance or credits are forfeited.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid up to 1 year from acceptance/activation; activation window is 30 days post-acceptance; inactive accounts (3 months without usage) get a 30-day warning before removal; unused credits do not roll over.
  - *Gotchas:* Partner-referral-only — no direct application path, which excludes bootstrapped/unaffiliated startups; 'up to $100k' is a ceiling, actual awards vary; credits apply to services, not products you can't resell; starting a trial during review is required by the process; three-month inactivity rule can void remaining credits.
  - *Source:* https://www.datadoghq.com/startups/
- **Student/education license (status: no public offer found as of 2026-10-22)** (medium) — No public Datadog education program, student license, or .edu-based free tier was found on the official site today: datadoghq.com/education/ returns 404; /solutions/education/ is a sales page aimed at selling Datadog TO institutions (Harvard Medical School, University of Chicago, Coursera as customers), with no free seat grants; /learn/ offers a free 'Learning Center' (hands-on course platform for all users, free of charge) but no student/educator account or seat grants, no .edu email requirement, and no classroom environment program is advertised (the older 'Datadog Classroom' URL structure is no longer live).
  - *Eligibility:* Not applicable — no public application process. The general 14-day trial and 5-host free plan are open to students/educators like anyone else (no CC required for trial; no CC stated for free plan), and free learning courses at /learn/ are open to all users.
  - *How to claim:* N/A — no education application exists. Nearest path: use the general free trial (no CC) + 5-host free plan for coursework; use free /learn/ courses for certification-style study.
  - *Expiry:* N/A for the (absent) program; free plan and trial terms as in their own findings.
  - *Gotchas:* Students have no dedicated .edu offer or classroom org grants discoverable today; do not assume a 'Datadog Classroom' free lab still exists; confirm with Datadog sales/academics if a class agreement is needed — any such access would be custom, not public.
  - *Source:* https://www.datadoghq.com/solutions/education/

### Docker

- **Docker Personal plan — $0 free tier for individual developers (incl. students)** (high) — Docker Personal is $0/user/mo with no stated time limit (self-serve at app.docker.com/signup). Includes: Docker Desktop (Engine + built-in Kubernetes, CLI, Compose), Docker Hub (unlimited public repos, 1 private repo, 100 image pulls/hr per authenticated user; 10 pulls/hr per IP unauthenticated), Docker Scout (1 Scout-enabled repo with continuous vulnerability analysis), Docker Debug, and 100 Testcontainers Cloud runtime minutes/month. Cap: 1 user. Free account gives 7-day trials of Docker Build Cloud and Testcontainers Cloud.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to any individual (no .edu email or student verification required). 'For individual developers who need the essential tools to build and deploy containers.' Does not cover company/team use except the small-company Desktop carve-out below. Government entities may not use Docker Desktop free without a subscription. No region limits or credit-card requirement stated on the official pages.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a free Docker Personal account at https://app.docker.com/signup 2) Download Docker Desktop from desktop.docker.com (Windows/macOS/Linux installers) 3) Log in with the Personal account — Docker Hub/Scout/Debug entitlements apply automatically. No application, no .edu proof, no CC wording required.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry — permanent $0 tier; entitlements 'remain free but still subject to licensing terms' (Docker plans FAQ).
  - *Gotchas:* 1 user only; 100 pulls/hr and 1 private repo are tight for class-group work (unauthenticated 10 pulls/hr/IP). Personal excludes Docker Pro extras (synchronized file shares, 2 Scout repos, unlimited Hub pulls, 100 Testcontainers min are the Personal/Pro boundary). If your company grows past the small-startup carve-out you must buy Team ($16/user/mo monthly, $15 annualized, up to 100 users) or Business ($24). Trial vs free: Build Cloud and Testcontainers Cloud are 7-day trials on Personal; only the 100 Testcontainers runtime minutes/month are permanently included.
  - *Source:* https://www.docker.com/pricing/
- **Docker Desktop free for early-stage startups (<250 employees AND <$10M revenue)** (high) — Per the Docker plans FAQ: 'For small companies with fewer than 250 employees AND less than $10M in revenue, Docker will continue to offer free usage of Docker Desktop only' — for (i) non-commercial open source projects and/or (ii) commercial use with <250 employees and <US$10,000,000 (or local-currency equivalent) annual revenue. This is a carve-out on top of the $0 Personal plan: the tool is free; the account entitlements are still the Personal ones.
  - *Eligibility:* Startup/company with <250 employees and <$10M USD (or equivalent local currency) annual revenue. Government entities excluded. No formal application, funding cap, or age cap stated — the caps are employee count and revenue. No region limits stated.
  - *How to claim:* No claim step beyond normal use: sign up at app.docker.com, install Docker Desktop (desktop.docker.com), and use it under the small-company terms. Self-declaration; keep company size/revenue under the caps and you remain free for Docker Desktop.
  - *Expiry:* No stated expiry; the carve-out 'will continue' per the current FAQ. It is revocable — crossing either cap means Docker Desktop is no longer free and a paid subscription (Team/Business) is required.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Docker Desktop only': no free entitlements for paid Hub features, Testcontainers Cloud minutes, Build Cloud, or support SLAs — the startup team still gets Personal-tier limits (100 pulls/hr/user, 1 private repo, 1 Scout repo). The 250-employee / $10M-revenue test can be outgrown mid-year with no grace period stated. Team plan starts at $16/user/mo once you exceed the carve-out.
  - *Source:* https://www.docker.com/pricing/faq/
- **7-day free trials: Docker Build Cloud and Testcontainers Cloud (via free Personal account)** (high) — Docker plans FAQ: 'Docker Personal users who want to start or continue using Docker Build Cloud may trial the service for seven days' and 'Docker Personal users may also trial Testcontainers Cloud for 7 days.' Without a paid subscription, a free Personal account gives the trial. Testcontainers Cloud is bundled into paid tiers (Pro 100 min/mo, Team 500, Business 1500 runtime minutes; overage buy-more at $3 per 100 min prepaid or $4 per 100 min on-demand). Open-source Testcontainers libraries (Java, Go, .NET, Node.js, Python, Rust, Ruby, PHP, etc.) remain free and open source.
  - *Eligibility:* Any individual with a free Docker Personal account (no .edu needed, no company requirement). No region limits stated.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Free Personal account at app.docker.com/signup 2) Sign up for Testcontainers Cloud trial at https://app.testcontainers.cloud/signup (or the in-product trial path) 3) Trial Build Cloud from Docker Desktop. 7-day windows; no stated duration limits beyond the 7 days.
  - *Expiry:* Trials last 7 days. No stated credit-card requirement on the official pages fetched (not mentioned).
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: only 100 Testcontainers Cloud runtime minutes/month are permanently included in Personal; the 7-day trial unlocks full service access, not the 100-min allotment. After trial, extra minutes cost $3-4/100 min. CI runs also consume runtime minutes — test-parallelism can burn minutes fast.
  - *Source:* https://www.docker.com/pricing/faq/
- **Docker Hardened Images (DHI) Community tier — free for every developer** (high) — Docker pricing page lists DHI 'Community' at $0: 'Free for every developer' — hardened minimal container images, near-zero CVEs, verifiable SBOMs, Apache 2.0 catalog. Paid tiers: DHI Select from $5,000 per repo (FIPS/STIG variants, critical-CVE fixes <7 days, up to 5 customizations) and DHI Enterprise (contact sales, unlimited customizations, +5-yr ELS updates). A 'Start Free Trial' CTA points to /products/hardened-images#getstarted.
  - *Eligibility:* Any developer/student/startup — no eligibility test, no .edu required, no CC requirement stated. 'Secure, transparent, and no-cost for everyone.'
  - *How to claim:* Pull DHI Community images from the Docker catalog, or start the DHI free trial via the 'Start Free Trial' link on the Hardened Images product page. No application.
  - *Expiry:* Community tier has no stated expiry. Trial of paid DHI has no stated duration on the pricing page.
  - *Gotchas:* Free tier is only the hardened image catalog (supply-chain tooling for the images themselves); it does not include Scout repo analysis beyond Personal's 1 repo, or the Select/Enterprise CVE-fix SLAs ($5k/repo).
  - *Source:* https://www.docker.com/pricing/
- **2025–26 change:** As of 2026-10-22, Docker has NO active student-specific program, education license, or startup program on its site. (1) 'Docker for Student Developers' (historically the .edu-verified student program, applied via docker.com/dsdev) now 404s and is not linked anywhere on docker.com — it appears sunseted/removed; the 404s are observed today, but no official end date was found (medium confidence it is dead, low confidence on when it ended). (2) The Y Combinator startup partnership (docker.com/ycombinator and /partners/y-combinator both 404 today) is not listed on the current /partners/ page, which now lists only Channel, Alliance, Extension, and Docker Trusted Content programs — so Docker's YC/startup deal is not publicly available (medium confidence). (3) Legacy 'Free Team' tier (up to 3 seats, 100 pulls/hr/user, no Build Cloud/Testcontainers Cloud) is referenced in the pricing FAQ with an upgrade path dated Dec 10, 2024; the current Personal plan is a 1-user $0 tier. (4) No trial credits in dollars, no funded startup accelerator benefits, and no classroom/educator license exist; Docker's free 'education' is limited to self-guided docs learning paths and free YouTube content (the LinkedIn Learning Docker Foundations Professional Certificate requires a paid LinkedIn subscription). Note: WebSearch was unavailable (session budget exhausted), so 2025-2026 change verification relied on official docker.com and testcontainers.com pages fetched today; confidence in sunset claims is therefore capped at medium.

### Figma Education

- **Figma Starter plan (free tier, open to everyone incl. students and startups)** (high) — Permanent free plan: unlimited draft files shareable with unlimited viewers; co-editing with unlimited users limited to 3 files (3 pages per file); UI kits and templates; 150 AI credits/day capped at 500 AI credits/month; Figma Buzz beta available. 'We do not have any discounts for non-profits. Anyone is welcome to use Figma's free plan.' No free trials of paid plans exist; Figma directs all startups to this free Starter plan.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to all individual users, no verification. No .edu email, no funding cap, no application. No credit card is mentioned or required for a free account (payment methods listed only for paid plans). No stated region limits. Credit-card requirement: not required for the free plan (FAQ silent; sign-up is email-only).
  - *How to claim:* Create a free account at figma.com (email signup). No claim step, no form, no partner referral. Account lands on Starter automatically.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; limits (3 co-edit files, 3 pages/file, 500 AI credits/mo, 150/day) apply continuously. AI credits reset monthly and do not roll over.
  - *Gotchas:* Freemium, not unlimited: >3 co-editable files or >3 pages/file forces upgrade; advanced Dev Mode inspection, team libraries, MCP server and Figma Make workflows are paid-plan features (Starter gets basic access only). AI credits: 150/day daily cap + 500/month cap, no purchase option on Starter — hitting the cap blocks paid-tier AI features until the next month. This is the fallback Figma recommends to startups whose dedicated program was discontinued.
  - *Source:* https://www.figma.com/pricing/andhttps://www.figma.com/pricing-faq/
- **Figma for Education: free Professional plan for verified students & educators (age 13+)** (high) — 'Figma & FigJam are 100% free for students, educators and schools.' Higher-ed, approved bootcamps, and secondary/high school students and educators get free Professional-tier access: unlimited files/projects, team-wide design libraries, advanced Dev Mode inspection and MCP Server. AI: 'Eligible education users get full access to Figma's AI toolkit, including Figma Make and AI-powered tools on the canvas' — but AI tools, the Figma agent, and Figma Sites are restricted to higher-ed and bootcamp Education users (not K-12/high-school); Education seats get 3,000 AI credits/month (per person, monthly reset, no rolver). Figma Sites: each eligible user may publish one self-created Site file; custom domains not available. No way to purchase extra AI credits on the Education plan — overage requires upgrading to paid Professional.
  - *Eligibility:* Current student or educator in a structured classroom (high school, university, post-grad, trade school, polytechnic, college, select curriculum-based online courses/bootcamps/workshops, design & coding academies); must be old enough to consent personally (13+ self-serve path); school-issued email REQUIRED — 'Non-school emails will be auto-rejected'; not strictly .edu (any school-issued email; personal accounts must switch to the school email before applying). Higher-ed verification goes through SheerID; bootcamp/online-course users cannot self-verify — the bootcamp admin applies as a trusted partner and submits user lists. Excluded: school staff in non-teaching roles, self-directed learners, startups/edtech companies, non-profits, and programs whose parent org's primary purpose is not educational. 'Figma for Education is only available in select regions and may not be accessible everywhere' (no published country list).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Have/switch Figma account to a school-issued email; sign up selecting School > role (Student/Educator) > institution type (High school / Higher Education / Bootcamp or online course). 2) 'Verify your status' under the Professional plan card at Figma.com/education/apply — high school: agree to terms and submit; higher ed: complete SheerID form; bootcamp: admin applies on your behalf. 3) A free Education team is created. K-12 districts / under-13 use instead: school contacts Figma and signs school terms (apply at /education/k-12/#contact).
  - *Expiry:* Per official validity table: high-school students 1 year; high-school educators 2 years; higher-ed students 1 year; higher-ed educators 2 years; bootcamp students 6 months; bootcamp educators 2 years; K-12 set by the domain administrator. Renewal: reapply for education status at term end. On lapse, team members drop to view-only and the team converts to a free Starter team (locked if it holds 4+ files) until re-verification or paid upgrade.
  - *Gotchas:* Education status is tied to the verified email — changing the account email revokes it. AI tools/Figma Make/Figma Sites unavailable to K-12 and high-school Education users (higher-ed and bootcamp only); even eligible users lack some AI features (e.g., visual search in editor). 3,000 AI credits/month is a hard per-person cap with no top-up on Education — hitting it blocks credit-based AI features until monthly reset. UK/Japan note: Figma may only be used by students above the age of consent; younger students require the district process. Data hosted on US servers; only email + name collected. Bootcamp educators stay free 2 years while students only 6 months.
  - *Source:* https://www.figma.com/education/andhttps://help.figma.com/hc/en-us/articles/360041061214-Figma-for-Educationandhttps://help.figma.com/hc/en-us/articles/37611757713431-AI-credits-on-the-Education-plan
- **Figma for Education: free Enterprise plan for K-12 districts (under-13 path)** (high) — K-12 districts 'qualify for free access to the Enterprise tier' — the most premium plan: custom team workspaces, design system theming and APIs, SCIM seat management, enterprise-level security. Provided at no cost via school-signed terms, replacing paid Enterprise (listed at $90/mo Full seat for commercial customers).
  - *Eligibility:* K-12 schools/districts intending to use Figma with students under 13; the school must be the applicant — individual self-serve not available. In UK and Japan, Figma 'may only be used by students above the age of consent', so under-13 use there requires this district route.
  - *How to claim:* School/district contacts Figma and signs the terms for schools; started via the form linked at /education/k-12/#contact ('Apply for K-12 program'). Domain administrator then manages the K-12 education status. Optional supports: bi-weekly free live training sessions and free on-site PD sessions for schools.
  - *Expiry:* K-12 education status duration is 'set by the domain administrator' — institution-controlled, not a fixed consumer term.
  - *Gotchas:* Not self-serve; K-12 users do NOT get AI tools, Figma Make, or Figma Sites (higher-ed/bootcamp only). The Education FAQ answers COPPA/FERPA/GDPR compliance by restating the age gate and school-signature process rather than a flat 'yes' per jurisdiction — compliance review needed for districts. US data residency; Figma deletes personal data within 30 days of request; Google Workspace/Microsoft Education SSO and classroom integration (FigJam Classroom add-on) available for managed rollouts.
  - *Source:* https://www.figma.com/education/andhttps://help.figma.com/hc/en-us/articles/360041061214-Figma-for-Education
- **Figma for Startups program — DISCONTINUED; no live offer as of 2026-10-22** (high) — The dedicated startup program (historically: free Organization plan for 24 months for VC-backed/accelerator-referred startups, or '$1,000 in Figma credits' per aggregator listings; one listing described 'up to 100% off Professional for 1 year, or 50% off for 6 months, new customers that raised under $10M') no longer exists on Figma-owned surfaces. figma.com/startups returns HTTP 404 (verified by direct fetch on 2026-10-22); no application form, no help-center article, and no startup mention in the pricing pages or pricing FAQ. Third-party trackers confirm: 'We could not verify this program on any Figma-owned page as of September 13, 2026' and 'No. Figma has no startup program: figma.com/startups is a 404, and Figma points everyone to its free Starter plan' (checked 2026-10-14).
  - *Eligibility:* None currently. Historical eligibility (per aggregators, now stale): VC-backed or accelerator-affiliated startup, referral by an approved Figma startup partner, new Organization-customer, Series A or below, <50 employees, raised under ~$10M. Note: early-stage startups are explicitly excluded from the Education plan ('Figma does not provide free Education teams for: Early stage Startups').
  - *How to claim:* No active claim path. Startups' only official free route is the unlimited free Starter plan. Accelerator/VC perk books may still republish the old offer, but no working application link exists on Figma-owned domains as of today.
  - *Expiry:* N/A — program discontinued; figma.com/startups 404 as of 2026-09-13 and re-verified 2026-10-22.
  - *Gotchas:* Aggregator sites (foundery.space, creditforstartups.com, startupintros.com, startup-perks.com) still list 'Figma for Startups' as Active with old terms — those listings are stale, not evidence the offer exists; always confirm on figma.com. Figma discounts do not stack — historically the largest single qualified discount applied. No nonprofit discount of any kind (FAQ: 'We do not have any discounts for non-profits'), so aggregator claims of '40-80% off nonprofit' are also stale.
  - *Source:* https://www.figma.com/pricing-faq/(startupexclusion+free-planstatement),https://www.figma.com/startups/(404),https://startupperks.co/programs/figma-for-startups,https://guptadeepak.com/startup-offers/programs/figma-for-startups
- **2025–26 change:** Figma for Startups: sunset/paused — figma.com/startups 404, no application form, no help-center article, confirmed unverified by multiple independent trackers Sept-Oct 2026; the program previously offered ~24 months of the free Organization plan (or ~$1,000 in credits) for VC/accelerator-referred startups and should not be counted as a live offer. Related 2025 sunset: Figma's affiliate program was deprecated in early 2025 (no new applications). Material 2025-2026 changes: paid plan pricing and seat structure changed (now Full/Dev/Collab seats: Professional $16/$12/$3 per month per seat, Organization $55/$25/$5, Enterprise $90/$35/$5 annual billing; Professional was ~$18/editor and Organization ~$35 pre-restructure) — do not quote old per-editor prices. AI credits are now built into every plan/seat (Starter 500/mo with 150/day cap; Pro Full 3,000/mo; Org Full 3,500/mo; Enterprise Full 4,250/mo; all other seats 500/mo) and Figma explicitly states there are no free trials of paid plans and no nonprofit discounts. New products included in seats (Figma Make, Motion, Sites, Buzz, Specs) are beta on paid seats. The Education program itself is live and current: free Professional for 13+ verified students/educators, free Enterprise for K-12 districts, 3,000 AI credits/month for higher-ed and bootcamp education seats, with 1-year (students) / 2-year (educators) / 6-month (bootcamp students) validity terms.

### Gitea

- **Self-hosted open-source Gitea (free tier)** (high) — The core Gitea release is open source under the MIT license and is free to run: 'Full control, unlimited users and repositories.' Bundles code hosting, issue tracking, pull requests, project management, Gitea Actions (GitHub-Actions-compatible CI/CD), and a package registry. Free features listed on the pricing page: code hosting, issue tracking, PRs, project management, Gitea Actions, packages, rapid updates, active contributions, community support. No repo/user/storage cap is published — you are limited only by your own infrastructure. This is the primary free path for students and startups: replace paid GitLab/GitHub tiers at $0 license cost.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to anyone: individuals, students, startups, and companies. No .edu email, startup-age or funding cap, and no credit card required. You must supply your own host (VPS/bare metal/K8s) and bear your own compute/data/egress costs.
  - *How to claim:* No application needed. Download the free binary or Docker image (gitea.com products page / github.com/gitea) and install on your own Linux/Windows server or VPS; configure a DB (SQLite/MySQL/PostgreSQL). Unlimited users/repos forever. Enterprise extras (SAML SSO, audit logs, K8s autoscaling runners, SLA support) are NOT in the OSS build.
  - *Expiry:* None — perpetual MIT license, no sunset date.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: this is a genuinely free, permanent license (not a trial). Self-hosted = you pay your cloud provider for CPU/egress; Gitea's license is free but the box is not. No managed CI runners are provided — OSS Gitea Actions requires you to run your own runners. Enterprise-only features absent; support is community-only.
  - *Source:* https://about.gitea.com/products/gitea
- **gitea.com — free first-party hosted Gitea instance** (medium) — Gitea operates gitea.com, a free public first-party Gitea instance: anyone can register a free account and host repositories on Gitea's own infrastructure at no charge. The sign-up form (gitea.com/user/sign_up) has only username/email/password plus OAuth (GitHub/GitLab/Google/OpenID) — no credit card or payment field. It is described as a 'free public first-party instance' hosting public repos (59 public repos on the Gitea org). No repo/storage cap is published on the page.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to everyone (individuals, students, startups). No .edu requirement, no startup/funding cap, no credit card required. Free to register.
  - *How to claim:* Register at gitea.com/user/sign_up, then create repos/projects. Hosts Gitea.org's own public open-source code (gitea/gitea, runner, tea CLI, etc.).
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing free service, subject to Gitea's terms; no published sunset date.
  - *Gotchas:* Free CI runners are NOT available on gitea.com (as of Nov 2025, maintainers confirmed there are no free managed runners; Gitea Actions needs a self-hosted runner or paid Gitea Cloud managed runners). The gitea.com root now 303-redirects to about.gitea.com (marketing); use gitea.com/<org>/<repo> for actual hosting. Exact repo/storage caps and private-repo policy are not published — treat as a public/mirror-friendly instance, not a guaranteed production private-code host. Source on no-free-runners: https://forum.gitea.com/t/how-to-make-gitea-actions-work-on-gitea-com/11793
  - *Source:* https://gitea.com/
- **Gitea Cloud — 30-day free trial (managed Git hosting)** (high) — Gitea Cloud is a fully managed, dedicated, single-tenant Gitea instance where you choose provider and region (incl. Frankfurt EU for data residency). A 30-day free trial is offered ('Start your 30-day free trial'); after it ends, it becomes pay-as-you-go (no permanent free hosted tier). Managed Gitea Actions CI/CD runners are included automatically. Unlimited network traffic: incoming traffic is free & unlimited; once the high-speed egress quota is hit, a speed cap applies only to outgoing traffic. A recurring 50% discount is available on most plans (Extra/Max/Ultra unlimited-user tiers excluded). Instances provision in ~2 minutes; public repos can allow anonymous access; 2FA supported.
  - *Eligibility:* Open to individual developers, startups, and enterprises — no .edu requirement and no dedicated startup program. No published funding/age cap. Payment is pay-as-you-go after the trial (credit card likely required at checkout; the 30-day trial itself is advertised without a stated CC requirement — not confirmed on the pages read).
  - *How to claim:* Go to cloud.gitea.com/signup → 'Start your 30-day free trial' → choose provider/region and a plan (S/M/L or Extra/Max/Ultra) → provision → use fully managed Gitea for 30 days → pay-as-you-go thereafter (apply the 50% discount where offered).
  - *Expiry:* Trial lasts 30 days, then pay-as-you-go. No published free-tier renewal; the 30-day trial is one-time.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a trial, not a permanent free tier — costs begin after 30 days (pay-as-you-go; add-on object storage is extra). Egress: high-speed quota then speed-capped outgoing (incoming free). 50% discount does not cover Extra/Max/Ultra tiers. Exact $/month per plan tier and the CC-required policy for the trial are not published in the pages read (Cloud site is an SPA). No free managed CI runners outside the trial.
  - *Source:* https://cloud.gitea.com/signup
- **Gitea Enterprise — 30-day free trial (self-hosted enhanced edition)** (high) — Gitea Enterprise is the enhanced, closed-source edition (SAML SSO, audit logs, Kubernetes AutoScaling Runners, mandatory 2FA, IP allowlist, enterprise themes, priority SLA support, installation/upgrade assistant, emergency remote support). A 30-day free trial is offered via the customers portal with full Enterprise features. List price is $19/user/month, with a recurring 50% discount shown as $9.50/user/month; a 1-year commitment is required with online payment. Licenses are user-count based and can be resized at any time; after a trial you can keep paying or revert to open-source Gitea (fully data/config compatible).
  - *Eligibility:* For companies/enterprises (and 'small companies' — discounts available for non-profits and small companies; FAQ notes discounted or even free quotes for education/non-profit on request). Not a dedicated free startup program. No .edu requirement for the trial itself; paid tier needs online payment (credit card) with a 1-year commitment.
  - *How to claim:* Go to customers.gitea.com → sign in with an organization account → 'Request Trial' → specify desired user count → a 30-day trial license is auto-issued → download license.key → in Gitea Enterprise admin: Site Administration → Enterprise License → Upload License. During the trial all Enterprise functions are free.
  - *Expiry:* Trial = 30 days. Paid licenses run for the committed term (typically 1 year, resizable), renewable; if unpaid after trial the instance falls back to open-source Gitea behavior (no data loss).
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-paid: free only for 30 days, then $19/user/month list ($9.50 with the 50% recurring discount) on a 1-year commitment. Exceeding the user count just blocks new sign-ups (resize for free). Resale of Gitea Enterprise is prohibited. Non-profit/small-company discounts and even free quotes require contacting Gitea — they are not self-serve.
  - *Source:* https://docs.gitea.com/enterprise/
- **Gitea Education Program — free 12-month Gitea Enterprise license (institutions)** (high) — Eligible non-profit K-12 and higher-education institutions receive a no-cost Gitea Enterprise license for 12 months, self-hosted, with community technical support only (no commercial support / no SLA). Renewable annually after an eligibility review. Covers coursework, labs, capstones, student group projects, non-commercial academic research, and internal school collaboration tied to the academic mission.
  - *Eligibility:* Institution-level, NOT individual students. Eligible: non-profit K-12 schools, colleges, and universities officially recognized/authorized/accredited/licensed or otherwise approved by the relevant education authority. Applications must come from full-time faculty/staff using an institutional email. Excluded: for-profit training firms, commercial bootcamps, private coaching, unrecognized/unaccredited organizations, and requests from students, alumni, or external consultants. Use is limited to academic/non-commercial — no commercial software delivery, paid hosting, or revenue-generating services. A .edu address is not strictly required, but an institutional email from a recognized non-profit institution is; proof of recognition/accreditation is required.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the contact form at about.gitea.com/contact/form/?program=education. Provide: institution name/website/country, a link or document showing education-authority recognition/accreditation, non-profit confirmation, applicant name/role/institutional email, expected user count + short usage summary, and acknowledgement that only community support is included. Every request is manually reviewed; on approval you get a tailored 12-month Enterprise license.
  - *Expiry:* 12 months, renewable annually after re-confirming eligibility and usage scope. Notify Gitea if eligibility or usage changes.
  - *Gotchas:* Students cannot apply directly — they benefit only via their school's license. Community support only (no commercial/SLA support). Non-commercial academic use only; commercial/resale/revenue use is prohibited and could void the license. No fixed seat count — scoped to the institution's expected users.
  - *Source:* https://about.gitea.com/education/
- **2025–26 change:** try.gitea.io — the free hosted "try" instance listed in the known-so-far notes — no longer resolves (DNS getaddrinfo ENOTFOUND on two attempts as of 2026-10-22), indicating it has been retired or repurposed; no current official page or terms could be found. Treat it as defunct. gitea.com's root domain now 303-redirects to about.gitea.com (marketing site); the free first-party public instance still serves orgs/repos under gitea.com/<org>/<repo>, but gitea.com no longer offers free managed CI runners (confirmed by a Gitea maintainer on the forum, Nov 2025 — Gitea Actions on gitea.com needs a self-hosted runner or paid Gitea Cloud managed runners). No dedicated Gitea startup program with free credits exists as of 2026-10-22 (startups' free path = self-hosted OSS Gitea + the Gitea Cloud 30-day trial). Recurring 2024→2026 changes still in effect: Gitea Cloud added a Frankfurt (EU) region, add-on object storage, unlimited network traffic (incoming free, outgoing speed-capped past a high-speed quota), and a 50% recurring discount on most plans; Enterprise lists at $19/user/month with a 50% discount to $9.50/user/month on a 1-year commitment. The Education Program (free 12-month Enterprise license for non-profit institutions) is current and live on about.gitea.com/education. Note: WebSearch budget was exhausted, so verification relied on WebFetch of official Gitea pages (about.gitea.com products/pricing/education, gitea.com, docs.gitea.com/enterprise, cloud.gitea.com) plus one forum confirmation.

### GitHub

- **GitHub Copilot Free (perpetual free tier, no credit card)** (high) — $0/month, no subscription or payment/credit card required. Includes up to 2,000 code completions/month, a (not numerically disclosed) allowance of GitHub AI Credits (1 AI credit = $0.01 USD), limited chat and agent usage via auto model selection only, Copilot CLI and the Copilot app. Under the legacy request-based system this was 50 premium requests/month; after the 2026-06-01 migration to AI-credit usage billing GitHub does not publish a fixed credit figure for the Free plan. Not available to users whose account is managed by an org/enterprise; personal use only. Never expires; limits reset monthly.
  - *Eligibility:* Any individual with a GitHub account not managed by an organization/enterprise and without an existing Copilot paid/Student/teacher/maintainer plan. No email domain, age, or region criteria stated. No credit card required. Region-limited only where Copilot is unavailable (standard Copilot availability restrictions apply).
  - *How to claim:* Sign up directly in VS Code/Visual Studio ('Sign up for Copilot Free'), via github.com/settings/copilot ('Start using Copilot Free'), or chat at github.com/copilot. No verification steps.
  - *Expiry:* Perpetual; monthly caps reset. Free/Student/teacher plans are reevaluated only where verification applies (Free has no verification, so no expiry).
  - *Gotchas:* Auto model selection only — no manual model picker, entry-level models only. Hitting the cap prompts an upgrade to Pro ($10). If you later join an org that assigns Copilot, Free access is lost. No code review, cloud agent, app modernization, or third-party agent delegation included. 'Unlimited completions' applies only to paid plans; Free caps at 2,000.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/features/copilot/plans
- **GitHub Copilot Student (free education license for verified students)** (high) — Free for students verified through GitHub Education: unlimited code completions and next-edit suggestions (completions never consume credits), plus a monthly GitHub AI Credits allowance — community-reported at 200 credits/month (~$2.00 of agent/chat usage) since the 2026-06-01 credit-based billing; GitHub does not publish a single official dollar figure for students, so verify in your plan's usage display. Limited chat/agent usage, auto model selection only. Since 2026-03-12 students are on a dedicated 'Copilot Student plan' (UI shows it alongside GitHub Education benefits); premium models incl. GPT-5.4, Claude Opus and Sonnet are no longer self-selectable (GPT-5.3-Codex removed from the picker 2026-04-27); access to OpenAI/Anthropic/Google models continues via Auto routing. Eligibility is reevaluated monthly by GitHub.
  - *Eligibility:* Students aged 13+ enrolled in a degree- or diploma-granting course at an accredited institution. Verification is via school-issued email and/or official, dated proof of enrollment (no strict .edu requirement, but .edu helps). No credit card required. Global program (thousands of universities). Not available to managed org accounts.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at github.com/settings/education/benefits ('Start an application', complete verification — school email/proof). Once verified, under 'Free GitHub developer resources for students and teachers' click Learn more and activate Copilot Student (or github.com/github-copilot/free_signup). Activation can lag verification by a few days; do not complete a paid checkout while waiting.
  - *Expiry:* Persists while academic verification remains valid; GitHub reevaluates eligibility every month — access is withdrawn when no longer enrolled/verified. Model/limit adjustments are being rolled out in stages (GitHub said more model/limit changes would follow the 2026-03 transition).
  - *Gotchas:* Credit allowance (~200 credits / ~$2) is small: agent-mode sessions with newer models can exhaust it in one day (community reports of 4-20 agent requests using the full allocation post-June 2026). Auto model selection only, no manual picker. No third-party agents, code review, or cloud agent included. ~2M students use the program; GitHub is actively trimming it for sustainability.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/copilot/how-tos/manage-your-account/free-access-with-copilot-student
- **GitHub Copilot Pro free for verified teachers and open-source maintainers (education/OSS license)** (high) — Full Copilot Pro at $0: unlimited completions, model selection, chat/agent/CLI, code review and cloud agent, and a $15/month AI-credit allowance (Base 1,000 + Flex 500 = 1,500 AI credits, 1 credit = $0.01). Two qualifying paths: (1) verified teacher on GitHub Education, (2) maintainer of a popular open-source repository (eligibility is determined by GitHub; objective repo thresholds are not publicly published — GitHub directs you to Copilot settings to see if you qualify).
  - *Eligibility:* Teachers: faculty at an accredited educational institution, verified via GitHub Education. OSS maintainers: maintainers of 'popular' open-source repos as judged by GitHub's criteria. Eligibility reevaluated monthly by GitHub.
  - *How to claim:* Teachers: apply/verify at github.com/settings/education/benefits, then activate in Copilot settings ('Get access to GitHub Copilot'). Maintainers: open Copilot settings; if eligible, a 'GitHub Copilot Pro' eligibility page appears — click Get access and save use policies. No payment info required for the free grant.
  - *Expiry:* While eligibility holds (monthly reevaluation). Note: if you are granted this free access you cannot cancel the plan — only losing eligibility ends it.
  - *Gotchas:* 'Popular' OSS-maintainer criteria are opaque; if you don't see the offer you likely don't qualify. Free access is personal, not shareable to the team. If you later join an organization that assigns Copilot seats, the free grant may be displaced.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/copilot-on-github/set-up-copilot/enable-copilot/set-up-for-teachers-and-os-maintainers
- **GitHub Education / Student Developer Pack benefits bundled with Copilot Student** (high) — Verified students also get a free GitHub Pro account (unlimited private repos with advanced collaboration), 3,000 GitHub Actions minutes/month, up to 180 GitHub Codespaces core hours/month (plus ~20 GB Codespaces storage), 2 GB GitHub Packages storage, and the Student Developer Pack (dozens of third-party partner offers for professional dev tools). These are GitHub's education-license companions to Copilot Student; the Copilot benefit is claimed from the same GitHub Education portal.
  - *Eligibility:* Students aged 13+ enrolled in a degree- or diploma-granting program at an accredited institution; apply with school-issued email and/or official dated proof of enrollment. Check first whether your school is a GitHub Campus Program partner (in that case benefits may be pre-arranged). No credit card required.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via education.github.com / github.com/settings/education/benefits, complete verification, then claim offers under 'Free GitHub developer resources for students and teachers' in that portal.
  - *Expiry:* Tied to active student verification (reevaluated monthly). Codespaces hours/Actions minutes reset monthly; unclaimed Codespaces hours are lost, not banked.
  - *Gotchas:* Actions minutes and Codespaces hours are per-month allowances that reset — no rollover. Usage in class/teacher-owned repos bills the owning org instead. 180 core-hours figure is what the docs state ('up to 180 core hours'); exact storage figures come from the marketing page.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/education/students
- **Trial credits / startup program: none GitHub-side for Copilot** (medium) — No time-limited free trial of paid Copilot plans is offered on the current plans page (the $0 Free plan is the entry point, not a trial), and GitHub does not run a GitHub Copilot startup program, startup credit pool, or funded 'Copilot for startups' offer. The nearest adjacent program is Microsoft's Cloud Startups initiative (Azure credits for eligible startups; GitHub is a Microsoft company, but Copilot Free/Student/Pro are not part of it and no Copilot-specific startup benefit was found). Confirmed by absence across the official plans, docs, and education pages reviewed today; web-search budget was exhausted before independently verifying Microsoft Cloud Startups terms.
  - *Eligibility:* n/a — no GitHub Copilot startup offer exists to claim.
  - *How to claim:* n/a.
  - *Expiry:* n/a.
  - *Gotchas:* Do not expect 'startup credit' or 'Copilot Pro trial' offers that third-party activation/activation-marketplace sites advertise (e.g., shared-account 'Copilot activation' services) — those are unofficial and violate ToS. The free path is strictly Free plan, verified-student plan, or teacher/OSS-maintainer Pro.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/features/copilot/plans
- **GitHub Pro (free for students)** (high) — Free GitHub Pro while verified as a student: 3,000 GitHub Actions min/month, 180 Codespaces core-hrs/month, 2GB GitHub Packages, 20GB Codespaces storage. Core of the pack; most-featured benefit.
  - *Eligibility:* GitHub account owned by a student verified through GitHub Education (school/.edu email or enrollment proof). Must be enrolled; benefits renew while student status is maintained.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a GitHub account owned by the student. 2) Verify as a student at education.github.com (school email or enrollment docs). 3) Pro benefits are applied automatically to the account once verified; manage via the pack page.
  - *Expiry:* Continuous while verified; re-verify as enrollment changes. Not a fixed trial.
  - *Gotchas:* Actions minutes beyond 3,000/mo are billable; Codespaces overage beyond 180 core-hrs incurs charges. Benefits lapse if student verification lapses.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Copilot (Student plan)** (high) — Copilot Student plan included free: unlimited code completions, an allowance of GitHub AI Credits for chat/agent/CLI/Spaces, and limited chat/agent usage restricted to auto model selection. More generous than the base Copilot Free tier (which is 2,000 completions/mo + a limited AI-credit allowance, 1 credit = $0.01, models incl. Haiku 4.5 / GPT-5 mini, no CC required).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via GitHub Education (the pack). Student plan is a dedicated tier; verified students link to the student offer from the copilot features page.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Verify student status in GitHub Education. 2) Activate the Copilot Student plan from the pack / Copilot page. 3) Use in editor, CLI, web, or Claude Code/agents.
  - *Expiry:* Tied to student/enrollment status; renews while verified.
  - *Gotchas:* Student plan chat/agent is capped and limited to auto model selection (vs. Pro/Pro+ choice). 2026: from ~Apr 24 GitHub may use Free/Pro/Pro+ interaction data for model training unless you opt out; from Jun 1, 2026 code-review workflows start consuming Actions minutes.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Codespaces (free Pro-level)** (high) — Free Pro-level access to Codespaces anywhere in the account, as part of GitHub Pro for students (supports the 180 core-hrs/mo + 20GB storage bundle).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Included automatically with GitHub Pro once student-verified; create and run Codespaces directly.
  - *Expiry:* Continuous while verified.
  - *Gotchas:* Compute hours beyond the 180 core-hrs/mo are billed; large machine sizes burn hours faster. Idle machines keep accruing hours.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Pages** (high) — One site per account and per organization, plus unlimited project sites, free.
  - *Eligibility:* Any GitHub user; in the pack context available to verified students.
  - *How to claim:* Available by default; deploy a site from a repo (gh-pages) or project site from a repo settings tab.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing; free tier with standard bandwidth limits.
  - *Gotchas:* Standard free-tier bandwidth limits apply; no AI/LLM features here.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub Desktop** (high) — Open-source desktop Git client, free for everyone (not student-specific).
  - *Eligibility:* Any user; listed in the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Download and install from github.com/desktop; sign in with a GitHub account.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing, free open source.
  - *Gotchas:* Requires a working GitHub account; no trial/CC involved.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Microsoft Azure for Students (18+)** (high) — $100 Azure credit usable within 12 months, plus free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months (new customers), plus 65+ always-free services. (Live page today shows a single flat student tier, not the older 'monthly $-credit' structure some sources still cite.)
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time university students 18+; sign up with a school email; renew yearly while enrolled; no credit card required; one account per person.
  - *How to claim:* 1) From the pack, open the Azure for Students claim link. 2) Sign up with school email. 3) Receive $100 credit + free monthly service amounts; renew yearly.
  - *Expiry:* $100 credit valid 12 months; free monthly amounts for 12 months; renew while a student.
  - *Gotchas:* Beyond free amounts and the $100 credit, metered/egress charges apply. Region availability varies. Always-free tiers have their own caps (e.g. 750 B1 hrs, 32GB storage).
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students/
- **Microsoft Azure for Students (ages 13-17)** (medium) — For students aged 13-17: free access to a subset of Azure services (App Services, Functions, Notification Hubs, MySQL in-app, Application Insights, Azure DevOps). No credit card required.
  - *Eligibility:* Students aged 13-17 via GitHub Education; separate tier from the 18+ offer.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's 13-17 Azure link; no CC.
  - *Expiry:* Continuous while enrolled in the 13-17 tier.
  - *Gotchas:* Service subset only; not the full 18+ catalog; no $ credit tier for this age band.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Visual Studio Dev Essentials (incl. $200 Azure credit)** (medium) — Visual Studio Community + Pluralsight training, and '1 free year of Azure services with $200 Azure credit for the 1st month' as listed in the pack.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Dev Essentials link; sign in with the student/educational account.
  - *Expiry:* ~1 year of Azure services; credit for the first month.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit and free-service terms are set by Microsoft/VS and can change; metered overage beyond the free year is billable.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Azure for Startups (startup offer, not in SDC pack)** (high) — For the 'Startup' front: $1,000 Azure credits instantly with no application, and up to $5,000 total credits after business verification. Initial credits valid 90 days; post-verification credits valid 180 days.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage startup (business), NOT the student program; separate from the GitHub Student Developer Pack. Business verification required for the larger tier.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at Azure for Startups; instant $1,000 tier, then complete business verification for up to $5,000.
  - *Expiry:* $1,000 credits 90 days; up-to-$5,000 credits 180 days post-verification.
  - *Gotchas:* Not available to the SDC student pack; it is a distinct startup program with its own criteria and verification.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students/
- **Heroku** (medium) — $13 USD/month for 24 months (Heroku platform subscription discount).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack; valid payment/claim method required by Heroku.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Heroku link; create Heroku account and apply the 24-month offer.
  - *Expiry:* 24 months. A 24-month committed/discounted subscription, not free-forever.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a 24-month discounted subscription that can auto-continue past 24 months unless cancelled - read renewal terms. Add-ons and extra resources are billed separately.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Appwrite (Education plan)** (medium) — Education plan: 2 projects at Pro-equivalent limits ($40/month value) for 6 months.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the 6-month education plan through the pack's Appwrite link.
  - *Expiry:* 6 months.
  - *Gotchas:* After 6 months, the plan reverts to free-tier limits or paid; egress/overage beyond included limits can cost.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **LocalStack** (medium) — Free license to LocalStack's AWS emulator in a ready-to-use cloud environment.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the free LocalStack license through the pack link.
  - *Expiry:* Per LocalStack's student terms (verify).
  - *Gotchas:* Check current license/region availability; cloud instance may have free-tier resource caps.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Stripe** (medium) — $25 in Stripe credits to offset fees on Stripe Payments, Billing and other products.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the $25 credit through the pack's Stripe link.
  - *Expiry:* Credit validity per Stripe terms (verify).
