  - *Gotchas:* Credits offset fees only; Stripe also charges per-transaction fees on top of covered usage; verify credit expiry.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Clerk** (medium) — Free Clerk Pro plan while a student (auth/UI, more limits than free tier).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the free Pro plan through the pack's Clerk link.
  - *Expiry:* While student status is maintained.
  - *Gotchas:* Reverts to free/paid tier after student status ends; confirm no CC is required at signup.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Sentry** (medium) — Free tier: 50K errors, 100K transactions, 1GB attachments, 500 replays, Team features, 1-year limit (renewable).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Sentry link; renew annually while a student.
  - *Expiry:* 1 year, renewable while student.
  - *Gotchas:* Beyond the error/transaction/replay caps, ingestion is blocked or downgraded; renew or lose access.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **MongoDB Atlas** (medium) — $50 MongoDB Atlas credits, plus access to MongoDB Compass and MongoDB University incl. a free certification valued at $150.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the $50 credit + tools/cert through the pack's MongoDB link.
  - *Expiry:* $50 credit window per MongoDB terms (verify).
  - *Gotchas:* Atlas cluster costs beyond the credit/MB free tier and egress are billable; free shared clusters have storage/network caps.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Datadog** (medium) — Pro account including 10 hosts/servers, free for 2 years.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the 2-year Datadog Pro through the pack's Datadog link.
  - *Expiry:* 2 years; renew per Datadog terms.
  - *Gotchas:* Beyond 10 hosts or included log/metric volume, charges apply; 2-year term then re-evaluates.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Camber** (medium) — Free Student plan while enrolled: 40 CPU hours, 5 GPU hours, 50GB storage, 50 agent messages/month.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the Camber student plan through the pack's link.
  - *Expiry:* While enrolled as a student.
  - *Gotchas:* Hour/message caps; GPU hours are scarce - large jobs consume them quickly; overage billed after caps.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **CARTO** (medium) — Free upgraded account for 2 years: increased storage, real-time data, Location Data Services credits, premium features.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the 2-year CARTO account through the pack's link.
  - *Expiry:* 2 years.
  - *Gotchas:* Storage/usage caps within the upgraded plan; billing resumes past caps or term.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Zyte Scrapy Cloud** (medium) — 1 Free-Forever Scrapy Cloud unit: unlimited team members, projects, and requests; unlimited crawl time; 120-day data retention.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the free Scrapy Cloud unit through the pack's Zyte link.
  - *Expiry:* Free forever (genuinely free, not a trial).
  - *Gotchas:* One unit; data retention capped at 120 days; heavier/concurrent crawl beyond the single unit requires paid scale.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **New Relic** (medium) — Free New Relic while a student (pack lists it as ~$300/month value).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's New Relic link.
  - *Expiry:* While student status is maintained.
  - *Gotchas:* Data ingestion/overage beyond the free allotment is billed; reverts to paid after student status ends.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Namecheap (.me + SSL)** (medium) — 1-year .me domain registration + 1 free SSL certificate for 1 year.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Namecheap link; register the .me and SSL.
  - *Expiry:* 1 year; domain/SSL must be renewed at standard cost afterwards.
  - *Gotchas:* 1-year term - renews at full retail; watch for auto-renewal charges after the free year.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Name.com** (medium) — 1 free domain from 25+ extensions (.live, .studio, .software, .app, .dev, etc.); account must have a unique address.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Name.com link.
  - *Expiry:* Free 1-year domain; renew at cost afterwards.
  - *Gotchas:* Renewal at standard price; verify the exact eligible TLD list (25+) and the unique-address requirement.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **.TECH** (medium) — One standard .TECH domain free for 1 year.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's .TECH link.
  - *Expiry:* 1 year.
  - *Gotchas:* Renews at standard price after the free year; watch auto-renewal.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **JetBrains (student licenses)** (medium) — Free annual student subscription to JetBrains IDEs (IntelliJ IDEA, PyCharm, WebStorm, DataGrip, etc.); renewed annually.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack (or directly at JetBrains with .edu email / school email + student status).
  - *How to claim:* 1) Verify student status. 2) Claim via JetBrains (pack link) or jetbrains.com/student using school email. 3) Install IDE and sign in with the student license.
  - *Expiry:* Annual; renew each year while a student.
  - *Gotchas:* Student licenses are for learning, not commercial production work; re-verify each year; some enterprise features excluded.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Visual Studio Code coding packs** (medium) — Coding packs to download Java, Python, or .NET toolchains for VS Code.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Download the VS Code coding pack through the pack's link.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing (VS Code itself is free).
  - *Gotchas:* Toolchain downloads are just pre-bundled; the actual toolchains (e.g. commercial .NET) may have separate licensing.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Xojo** (medium) — Xojo Pro license free while a student (cross-platform desktop/mobile/web/Raspberry Pi dev).
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim the free Xojo Pro through the pack's link.
  - *Expiry:* While a student; reverts to paid after status ends.
  - *Gotchas:* Distributed app licensing may still apply when shipping commercial products.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Bootstrap Studio** (medium) — Free Bootstrap Studio license while a student.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.
  - *How to claim:* Claim through the pack's Bootstrap Studio link.
  - *Expiry:* While student status is maintained.
  - *Gotchas:* Reverts to paid after student status ends.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **Polypane** (medium) — Free Polypane individual plan for 1 year.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified student via the pack.

- **GitHub Pages free hosting tier (GitHub Free plan)** (high) — $0/mo flat-free hosting, no credit card required. Limits: up to 1 user or organization site per account plus unlimited project sites; recommended source-repo limit 1 GB; published site size capped at 1 GB; soft bandwidth limit of 100 GB/month; soft build limit of 10 builds/hour (this cap is waived when you build/publish via a custom GitHub Actions workflow); deployments time out after 10 minutes. Sites that hit rate limits return HTTP 429. Custom domains and HTTPS are supported at no cost. Pages on public repos is available on GitHub Free; hosting Pages for private repos requires GitHub Pro/Team/Enterprise (or the free student tier).
  - *Eligibility:* Any personal or organization GitHub account. GitHub Free is $0 with no credit card. Public Pages sites work on GitHub Free (public repos). Private-repo Pages requires GitHub Pro/Team/Enterprise (paid plans) OR free GitHub Education student status. No founding-age, funding, or geographic restriction on the free tier.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Create a free GitHub account (no card). 2) For a user/org site name the repo <user>.github.io or <org>.github.io; for project sites use any project repo. 3) Add an entry file at the source root (index.html / index.md / README.md). 4) In repo Settings > Pages, choose the publishing source (branch/folder or a custom Actions workflow). 5) Click 'Visit site' to see the live deployment; add a custom domain + HTTPS under Pages settings if needed.
  - *Expiry:* Indefinite: part of the free GitHub plan; no expiry while the account/plan stay active. GitHub can change limits at any time without notice; no 2025-2026 sunset of the free tier observed.
  - *Gotchas:* 1 GB site size and 100 GB/mo bandwidth are SOFT limits, not hard caps: exceeding them can cause the site to stop being served or trigger an email asking you to reduce server impact. 10 builds/hour is a soft cap and 10 min is a build timeout. No separate egress/data-egress charge on the free plan beyond the soft bandwidth cap. On GitHub Free, the repo hosting the Pages site must be public; private Pages pushes you to a paid plan unless you have free student status.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/pages/getting-started-with-github-pages/github-pages-limits
- **GitHub Education / Student Developer Pack (education license)** (high) — Verified students get GitHub Pro FREE (unlimited private repos with advanced collaboration tools), free GitHub Pages hosting (incl. for private repos), GitHub Copilot Student (free access to Copilot and its premium features), GitHub Codespaces up to 180 core hours/month with Pro-equivalent storage (~20 GB Codespaces storage and ~2 GB Packages storage), ~3,000 GitHub Actions minutes/month, and roughly 80 third-party 'Student Developer Pack' partner offers (e.g., a free domain, ~$100 Azure credit, JetBrains, 1Password). Student validation lasts 2 years.
  - *Eligibility:* 13 years or older; enrolled in a degree- or diploma-granting program (high school, college, university, or homeschool; bootcamps qualify only if part of GitHub's Campus Program). Requires a personal GitHub account. Verify with a school-issued email OR dated proof of enrollment (school ID photo, class schedule, transcript, or affiliation/enrollment letter showing a date and current status) — a .edu email is NOT strictly required. No credit card required. No GitHub-imposed regional cap, though individual third-party partners may add their own age/region limits. Teachers/faculty/staff get free/discounted GitHub but are NOT eligible for the Pack.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Sign in to the personal GitHub account you want benefits on. 2) Add/verify your school email under Settings > Emails (or rely on uploaded enrollment proof if your school issues no email). 3) Make your profile name match your documents. 4) Go to Settings > Education benefits, under 'GitHub Education' click 'Start an application'. 5) Fill the form, select your school, upload dated proof of enrollment, submit. 6) After approval (usually a few days), open the GitHub Education portal / Student Developer Pack page and claim each offer individually. No credit card needed.
  - *Expiry:* Student validation lasts 2 years, then must be re-validated. GitHub Pro, Pages, Copilot Student, and Codespaces access continue only while you remain a verified, enrolled student. Each third-party partner offer carries its own terms and duration.
  - *Gotchas:* The third-party partner offers (free domain, Azure credits, JetBrains, 1Password, etc.) are the de-facto 'trial credits' — they churn, pause, or are removed at the partner's request (the 2024-10 partner list already removed Netwise, Typeform, and a .Tech email offer). You cannot re-redeem a time-limited offer by re-verifying student status; some partners disallow stacking. Individual partners may impose age/country limits. The 180 Codespaces hours/month are for your personal account; Codespaces usage in GitHub Classroom assignment repos is billed to the classroom org, not you. The free-domain offers are typically time-limited (often 1 year). GitHub Pro/Pages/Copilot/Codespaces access lapses when student status lapses.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub for Startups (startup program) — $10,000 Enterprise credits** (high) — Partners (VCs, accelerators, incubators) refer early-stage startups to receive $10,000 in GitHub credits usable across the Enterprise platform for up to 12 months, covering GitHub Copilot, GitHub Advanced Security, GitHub Actions (with 50k included minutes), and GitHub Packages. Also includes tailored onboarding, office hours, technical best-practice guidance, and entry into the GitHub startups community (~40,000 startups and 1,000+ ecosystem partners). This is NOT a free Pages hosting tier — Pages is already free to everyone; this grants Enterprise-platform credits. Program page carries © 2026 GitHub, Inc., so it is current as of 2026.
  - *Eligibility:* Must be referred/affiliated with a GitHub for Startups partner (VC, accelerator, or incubator; any such org can submit a partner application). Must have received outside funding up to Series B or earlier (non-equity credits do not count). Must be NEW to GitHub Enterprise (no Enterprise plan in the prior 6 months and no prior GitHub credits or Enterprise licenses). Enterprise Cloud on github.com only (Enterprise Server and Data Residency excluded). No founding-age cap, no company-size cap, and no geographic restriction stated. A payment method IS required (an authorization charge is expected within weeks of onboarding). One-time offer: previously credited accounts are ineligible.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Get referred by a VC / accelerator / incubator that is a GitHub for Startups partner (or have that org submit a partner application). 2) Alternatively reach out via the page's 'Contact sales' link. 3) Approval email arrives in ~2-3 business days. 4) Onboard to GitHub Enterprise Cloud on github.com and add a payment method (authorization charge expected). 5) Spend the $10,000 Enterprise credits across Copilot / Advanced Security / Actions (50k min) / Packages within 12 months. This is partner-gated (not open self-signup), requires a payment method, and is a credits grant, not free Pages hosting.
  - *Expiry:* Credits valid up to 12 months from approval — they expire on full consumption or at the end of the 12-month period, whichever comes first. Program active as of 2026 (© 2026 GitHub, Inc.).
  - *Gotchas:* Not a free-forever frontend hosting tier: it is a $10k Enterprise-credits grant. Partner referral is mandatory (not open application). A payment-method authorization charge is required despite the credit coverage. After credits are consumed or 12 months elapse, active products automatically transition to standard paid billing. Downgrading before the credits are exhausted forfeits the remaining credits AND bars reapplication. Enterprise Cloud on github.com only — not Enterprise Server or Data Residency. Funding cap: Series B or earlier. One-time offer only.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/enterprise/startups
- **GitHub Actions Free plan (available to everyone, incl. students & startups)** (high) — Verified today at docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-actions and github.com/pricing. Private repos: 2,000 CI/CD minutes/month and 500 MB artifact storage (pooled with GitHub Packages) on the Free plan; Pro is 3,000 min/1 GB, Team 3,000 min/2 GB, Enterprise Cloud 50,000 min/50 GB; cache 10 GB/repo. Public repos using standard GitHub-hosted runners: minutes are free/unlimited ('GitHub Actions usage is free for self-hosted runners and for public repositories that use standard GitHub-hosted runners'). Self-hosted runners: free. GitHub Pages and Dependabot runs: free. No credit card required for the free tier. Startups: there is no separate startup program — any startup just gets this free tier.
  - *Eligibility:* Everyone with a GitHub Free account; no .edu email, no age/region limit (min account age standard). Credit card NOT required.
  - *How to claim:* Already active by default on any GitHub Free account — no application needed. Usage is charged to the repo owner/billing account, not the workflow trigger.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; allowance resets monthly. Larger runners and excess usage beyond 2,000 private min are billed per minute (e.g. Linux 2-core $0.006/min, macOS $0.062/min) once you exceed the quota.
  - *Gotchas:* Standard-runner public-repo minutes are unlimited; larger/custom runners are always charged even in public repos. Artifact storage is a shared pool with GitHub Packages. Minutes charged to repo owner, so a team/organization shares the 2,000 min per account, not per person. One github.com/pricing rendering I fetched suggested 'Unlimited public repo minutes — Students only; all other users 2,000 min/mo public', which contradicts the docs fetched the same day ('public repositories ... free', no student-only language) — trust the docs and treat public-repo minutes as free for all.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-actions
- **GitHub Education for students (Student Developer Pack): free Copilot, Codespaces hours, free Pro** (high) — Verified today at docs.github.com/en/education/.../about-github-education-for-students, the apply page, the Student Developer Pack T&C page, and github.com/education. After verification: free GitHub Copilot 'Student' tier ('Verified students get free access to Copilot and its premium features'); GitHub Codespaces up to 180 core hours/month for personal accounts; Student Developer Pack with 80+ partner offers (cloud hosting to dev platforms to premium content, 'use professional tools at no cost'); the github.com/pricing education section lists free GitHub Pro for students/educators plus the same Codespaces/Copilot/pack benefits. GitHub Pro duration is not itemized in the current docs (historically 1 year; T&C do not restate it).
  - *Eligibility:* Student or faculty at an accredited educational institution, or enrolled in a degree/diploma-granting program (high school, college, university, or homeschool). Proof of enrollment: school ID with current date, class schedule, transcript, or enrollment verification letter. If your institution issues academic emails, you must add and verify one on your GitHub account (i.e. .edu/academic email where applicable; otherwise supply official documentation). Own a personal GitHub account; age 13+. No credit card required.
  - *How to claim:* Settings → Education benefits (github.com/education) → 'Start an application' under GitHub Education → fill form (proof of enrollment) → submit; verify academic email first. Check first whether your institution is already a GitHub Campus Program partner. Benefits appear in the Education portal after approval.
  - *Expiry:* Student validation lasts 2 years, then revalidation required. Offers cannot be redeemed multiple times (including via new accounts). Third-party partners can change or remove offers at will; GitHub says it will try to notify in advance. Codespaces 180 core hours is a recurring monthly allowance; Pro offer (historically) 1 year.
  - *Gotchas:* The current docs 'About' page no longer itemizes free GitHub Pro or Actions minute allowances — only Copilot, Codespaces 180 h, and the pack; free Pro was confirmed from the pricing page's education section, not the docs (medium confidence there). Pack is partner-dependent: offers vary, no stacking of promos, terms are student-vs-partner. Faculty/teachers get a separate track; a school club can get a free GitHub Team with unlimited users + private repos for free.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/education/about-github-education/github-education-for-students/about-github-education-for-students
- **GitHub Campus Program (education license for schools): free GitHub Enterprise** (high) — Verified today at docs.github.com/en/education/.../about-github-campus-program. Schools get GitHub Enterprise Cloud and GitHub Enterprise Server free of charge, free for one year with automatic annual renewal, 'so long as you continue to operate within the terms of the agreement'. The license bundles 50,000 GitHub Actions minutes and 50 GB GitHub Packages storage. This is the main 'education license' offer — it benefits students at partner schools by making Enterprise tooling available campus-wide.
  - *Eligibility:* Any school that grants degrees, diplomas, or certificates. Application must be signed by a school employee with authority to adopt technology school-wide (CTO/CIO, Dean, Department Chair). Internal IT, research groups, and other departments may use the licenses provided they do not profit from them. Students themselves do not apply.
  - *How to claim:* Apply through the Campus Program (referenced from the GitHub Education docs; application via the official Campus Program site / GitHub Education Resource Guide). Check institution status on the education docs before any individual student applies.
  - *Expiry:* Free for 12 months; auto-renews annually as long as terms are met.
  - *Gotchas:* For institutions, not individuals — individual students can't claim it. If your school is already a Campus partner, students at that school should not submit a separate individual application. Enterprise Server deployment implies self-managed infrastructure; no cost but operational burden on the school.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/education/about-github-education/use-github-at-your-educational-institution/about-github-campus-program
- **Copilot Free (no-credit-card free tier, all individual users)** (high) — $0 tier: 2,000 completions per month, 'an allowance of GitHub AI Credits' (page says 'Limited chat and agent usage'; no fixed credit number published on the marketing page; 1 AI credit = $0.01 USD). Auto model selection only (as of 2026-06-24 the only model-selection experience on Free/Student); agents 'Limited'; Copilot Chat skills not available; no app modernization, no cloud agent, no code review. New signups remain open even when paid plans were paused. Open to everyone at github.com/sign-up.
  - *Eligibility:* Individual developers who do NOT already have Copilot through an organization/enterprise. No credit card required, no trial. No region limit stated on the pages fetched.
  - *How to claim:* Sign up for GitHub -> choose Copilot Free (no card). Available from github.com/features/copilot/plans; IDEs/CLI/GitHub mobile included.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; monthly rolling limits. (Free tier itself: none published.)
  - *Gotchas:* Marketing page wording: 'No credit card required' appears on the Free card at github.com/features/copilot. Free users are 'limited to 2,000 completions and access to entry level models per month' — entry-level model access despite the full model list being shown in the table. If you have an org-provisioned Copilot you can't use Free on the same account.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/concepts/billing/individual-plans

Excludes third-party agents. Replaces the old free-access entitlement that had unlimited premium models.

GitHub reevaluates eligibility every MONTH. No credit card. New Student signups were paused 2026-04-20 and reopened from ~2026-06-16/17; as of 2026-10-22 signups are open.
  - *How to claim:* github.com/settings/education/benefits -> apply as student; after approval, 'Free GitHub developer resources for students and teachers' -> Learn more -> activate Copilot Student (activation can lag verification by days; direct URL github.com/github-copilot/free_signup). No action was needed for existing students at the 2026-03 transition.
  - *Expiry:* Rollover: monthly re-evaluation; 200 credits/month (community-discussion figure); unlimited completions.
  - *Gotchas:* '200 monthly AI credits' figure comes from the official community discussion of the transition, not a docs table — medium confidence on that number specifically. Monthly re-verification means losing eligibility (graduation) drops the benefit; the plan is not a paid subscription, so 'free plan runs out the moment you graduate'. Existing premium-request-unit (PRU) entitlements for pre-existing students were kept at transition. Student tier no longer includes premium-model manual selection — the biggest 2025->2026 change.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/copilot/how-tos/copilot-on-github/set-up-copilot/enable-copilot/set-up-for-students

Docs (plans page) phrase it as 'Verified teachers... may be eligible for free access to Copilot Pro' — a benefit, not a guaranteed signup. Pro signups were briefly paused (2026-04-20 to ~2026-06-16) during the billing restructure; free-for-teachers activation follows the same plan plumbing. Opus models were removed from Pro (2026-04-20) even in the free-teacher grant.

Free Pro = 1,000 base + 500 flex = 1,500 AI credits/mo (paid-plan equivalent; $15/mo worth base pool on marketing page).
  - *Eligibility:* Maintainer of a 'popular' project — the current pages do NOT document the popularity threshold (no stars/followers number published on github.com/open-source or the plans docs page; historically tied to project popularity, e.g. high star/follower counts). Claim is through the normal Copilot sign-up, where eligible accounts get Pro instead of paid checkout.

Benefit is not a paid subscription, but Pro plan mechanics (model restrictions, e.g. Opus removed from Pro 2026-04-20) apply. Pro signups were paused 2026-04-20 to ~2026-06-16.

- **GitHub for Startups program ($10,000 GitHub Enterprise Cloud credits, incl. Copilot)** (high) — Eligible startups receive $10,000 in GitHub credits applied to their Enterprise Cloud account, valid up to 12 months (expire on full consumption or 12-month end, whichever first). Credits cover Enterprise licenses, Copilot licenses/premium models/agentic capabilities, Advanced Security, Actions, and other metered offerings; includes 50k included Actions minutes under Enterprise tier. The page notes a PREVIOUS startup offer has been discontinued and is no longer available.
  - *Eligibility:* Must be tied to an approved partner (VC, accelerator, incubator); received outside funding 'up to Series B or earlier' (stage cap, non-equity credits don't count as funding); NEW to GitHub Enterprise — not on an Enterprise plan within the past 6 months and no prior credits/licenses; GitHub.com Enterprise Cloud only (no Enterprise Server/Data Residency). Pages fetched state NO founding-age cap, no dollar funding ceiling, and no geographic restriction.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Get referred/apply through a GitHub for Startups partner (partner directory + partner-application form on the page). 2) Submit the Enterprise application; applicant's primary email gets a reply within 2-3 business days. 3) Complete onboarding INCLUDING adding a payment method. A valid payment method (credit card) IS required — 'Failure to have an authorization... will result in your account being locked.'
  - *Expiry:* Credits: 12 months or full consumption; program: no end date published.
  - *Gotchas:* Payment method required (unlike all the offers above). One-time redemption; after credits expire/12 months, products 'automatically transition to standard paid billing'; violations can have credits revoked. No specific seat counts/Codespaces hours published on the page — the old 'X free seats' style offer was discontinued. Partner-gated: you cannot self-serve without a partner referral.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/enterprise/startups
- **GitHub Codespaces Free plan (all users)** (high) — Personal Free accounts include 120 core-hours of codespace compute + 15 GB of codespace storage per month (renew each billing cycle). A 2-vCPU codespace burns 2 core-hours per active hour, so 120 core-hours = 60 hours on 2 cores, 30 hours on 4 cores ($0.36/hr), 15 hours on 8 cores ($0.72/hr). 16/32-core sizes cost $1.44/$2.88 per hour. Storage overage: $0.07/GB-month; compute overage only if you add a payment method and raise the spend limit. Free accounts (github.com/pricing) have a $0 spend limit by default, so no CC is required and charges cannot accrue unless you opt in. No free quota exists for organization/enterprise accounts on the Free plan.
  - *Eligibility:* Any GitHub account on the Free plan (personal accounts only; 'GitHub Free for organizations' includes none). No credit card, no verification.
  - *How to claim:* Create a free GitHub account; codespaces work immediately in a web/VS Code session. Check usage at github.com/settings/billing (Codespaces section) or github.com/codespaces. No application or enrollment needed.
  - *Expiry:* Quota renews automatically each monthly billing cycle (based on personal billing day). No sunset of the free tier announced as of 2026-10-22.
  - *Gotchas:* When the 120 core-hours or 15 GB run out, creating/opening new codespaces is blocked unless you add a payment method and set a budget. Storage accumulation is cumulative within the cycle: deleting codespaces does not claw back current-month storage. Stopped codespaces auto-delete after ~30-day retention but count toward storage until then; prebuilds are disabled once storage is exhausted. Dev containers using default images do not count toward storage. Orgs on the Free plan have 0 included core-hours — a common trap.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/product-billing/github-codespaces
- **GitHub Free for students (free Pro via Student Developer Pack) — 180 core-hours + 20 GB** (medium) — Verified students get GitHub Pro free for the duration of their student status, which carries Pro-level Codespaces: 180 core-hours/month + 20 GB codespace storage (the Pro included-quota values read today from the Codespaces billing docs; students map to Pro, not a separate student codespace quota). Also included at no cost: full GitHub Pro plan, GitHub Copilot for students ('unlimited code completions + GitHub AI credits, limited chat/agent, auto model selection'), GitHub Pages limits, plus third-party partners (Azure $100 credit, JetBrains, Datadog, 1Password, etc.).
  - *Eligibility:* Enrolled students; verification through GitHub Education (education.github.com/pack). Typically a school-issued email (.edu or equivalent) or proof of enrollment; school/issuer photo ID may be used. No credit card required. No stated region limit. Teachers: verified academic faculty get GitHub Team free for teaching/research (separate offer, see sunset notes for plan-table changes).
  - *How to claim:* Sign in at education.github.com/pack -> verify student status with school email/ID -> pack benefits apply automatically account-wide (Pro plan + Codespaces Pro quota). No separate codespaces enrollment.
  - *Expiry:* Lasts 'while you are a student' (pack states the Pro benefit persists during enrollment; re-verification happens when status changes, effectively each school year). Included codespace quota resets monthly with the billing cycle.
  - *Gotchas:* The old dedicated 'Free for students' codespace tier (120 core-hours/15GB distinct from Pro) is no longer documented — students now receive Pro's 180 core-hours/20GB, so the figures are read via the Pro row. Pro included usage does not cover metered add-ons (Copilot usage, GHAS, etc.) — plan discounts don't apply to usage-based billing. Copilot student tier is 'limited' chat/agent — agent-heavy use exhausts quickly. Pack partner offers (Azure, Datadog) are trial/credit-based, not perpetual.
  - *Source:* https://education.github.com/pack
- **GitHub for Startups program — $10,000 credits (education/startup front: startup program)** (high) — Current startup offer (verified on github.com/enterprise/startups today): eligible startups get $10,000 in GitHub Enterprise platform credits usable across Enterprise, Copilot, Advanced Security, Actions (50,000 included Action minutes) and other metered add-ons (i.e. codespaces compute billed to the Enterprise org) for up to 12 months. Includes onboarding/office hours. This is a credits program on Enterprise Cloud, not a free codespaces tier for startups.
  - *Eligibility:* Company must have outside funding up to Series B or earlier (non-equity credits excluded); must be new to GitHub Enterprise (no Enterprise plan in prior 6 months and never received GitHub credits or Enterprise licenses); must apply through an approved partner (VC, accelerator, or incubator); GitHub Enterprise Cloud on github.com only (no Enterprise Server/Data Residency); one-time redemption.

  - *Gotchas:* A card authorization charge hits your payment method within weeks of onboarding (a failed charge locks the account) — so unlike the free tiers, this program effectively requires a payment method. Downgrading before credits are consumed forfeits the balance and bars re-application. All program/billing notices go to the primary account email. No free Pro/Team plan for startups is offered anymore — value is credit-based only.
  - *Source:* https://github.com/enterprise/startups
- **GitHub Team free for verified academic faculty (education license)** (medium) — Verified academic faculty can apply for GitHub Team free 'for teaching or academic research' — unlimited private repos and unlimited collaborators, per the discounted-plans docs. Team is a metered/usage-based plan, so faculty-driven codespaces on Team are usage-billed (no free core-hours included), making this more an IDE/CI license than a codespaces offer.
  - *Eligibility:* Verified academic faculty (school-affiliated accounts via GitHub Education); institutions/nonprofits route through the education/nonprofit portals.
  - *How to claim:* Apply through the GitHub Education benefits/verification flow (linked from docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/discounted-plans); faculty Team grant is account-level, no CC required on the free grant itself.
  - *Expiry:* Tied to active academic status (school year / employment).
  - *Gotchas:* No codespaces core-hour allocation with the free Team grant — codespaces usage bills against usage-based billing; nonprofit/library variant is free Team or 25% off Enterprise Cloud via nonprofits.github.com.
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/billing/concepts/discounted-plans
- **Free GitHub Pro plan (core student benefit)** (high) — The full personal GitHub Pro plan is free for the duration of student status. Current official limits per docs.github.com product-usage reference (fetched 2026-10-22): 3,000 GitHub Actions minutes/month, 180 GitHub Codespaces core hours/month + 20 GB Codespaces storage, 10 GB Git LFS, unlimited private repos, and Pro security/advanced features. No credit card required. NOTE: older 2024-25 materials state 5,000 Actions minutes and 50 GB Codespaces storage; those no longer match the current docs.

Not a one-off trial — recurring benefit tied to student status.

  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/billing/reference/product-usage-included
- **GitHub Codespaces (Pro tier, free for students)** (high) — Pro-tier Codespaces included with the free Pro plan: 180 core hours/month and 20 GB storage (vs 120 core hours + 15 GB on the standalone Free plan).   - *Gotchas:* Long-running, high-CPU, or large-storage codespaces burn through 180 core hours quickly and then cost real money (compute + egress/storage). '50 GB storage' from older sources is not current (docs show 20 GB).
  - *Source:* https://docs.github.com/en/billing/reference/product-usage-included

- **GitHub Actions minutes (free, via Pro)** (high) — 3,000 GitHub Actions minutes/month included on the free Pro plan (vs 2,000 on the standalone Free plan; 50,000 on Enterprise). Public-repository runs are free/unlimited on Free; private-repo runs draw down the allowance. Overage is metered.

(3) 2025-08: Free plan's chat allowance reduced from 200 to 50 premium requests/month. (4) 2026-03-12/13: existing student benefits were migrated to a dedicated 'Copilot Student plan' with auto model selection only; premium models (GPT-5.4, Claude Opus/Sonnet) removed from student self-selection — announced by GitHub Education in community discussion 189268; further model/limit adjustments were flagged as upcoming. (5) 2026-04-27: GPT-5.3-Codex removed from the Student model picker. (6) 2026-06-01: all plans moved to usage-based 'GitHub AI Credits' billing (1 credit = $0.01): Pro $15/1,500 cr, Pro+ $70/7,000 cr, Max $200/20,000 cr; student allowance is community-reported at ~200 credits/month; legacy per-request billing survives only for Pro/Pro+ annual-plan users who opted to stay. Sunset: the 'unlimited chat with premium models' framing of older student coverage is obsolete — the student plan is now credit-capped and auto-routing-only. No free offer was fully discontinued: Free, Student, and teacher/maintainer Pro all remain active as of 2026-10-22.

### GitLab

- **GitLab.com Free plan (permanent free tier)** (high) — Ongoing $0/month plan requiring no credit card. Included: 400 CI/CD compute minutes/month, applied per top-level group ("The quota will be applied to a group, no matter the number of users in the group or the number of users in the subscription"); initial 10 GiB limit per project on Git repo + LFS storage, which is "adjustable" (extra storage purchasable); 5 users per top-level group with private visibility ("The 5-user limit is applicable only to users of the Free tier of the GitLab.com offering in a top-level group with private visibility"). Basic security features on all tiers: SAST, secret detection, container scanning. No GitLab Duo credits included on Free (add-on, $1/credit on-demand; monthly commitment pools available to Free top-level group namespaces but not personal namespaces; compute-minute top-ups $10 per 1,000 minutes).
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone can sign up on gitlab.com, no CC required, no age/institution requirement. Public groups, paid tiers, and community-program users are exempt from the 5-user cap.
  - *How to claim:* Create a gitlab.com account (sign up at gitlab.com/users/sign_in), the group sits on the Free plan automatically; purchase add-ons (storage/minutes) via group billing settings.
  - *Expiry:* Ongoing; no sunset date published. Overage add-ons bill monthly (storage $5/mo per 10 GiB on paid tiers; minutes $10/1,000).
  - *Gotchas:* 400 minutes is a hard per-group per-month cap for CI/CD on shared runners; public groups are the main workaround for the 5-user cap; Duo/AI features are paid add-ons even on Free; self-managed GitLab CE (open-source edition, $0) has no user cap and no 400-minute cap (BYO runners/storage) — see https://about.gitlab.com/install/.
  - *Source:* https://about.gitlab.com/pricing/
- **GitLab for Education (free Ultimate license for educational institutions)** (high) — Complimentary GitLab Ultimate license, SaaS or self-managed, with UNLIMITED seats and 50,000 compute minutes/month included (extra minutes purchased separately). 1-year duration, renewed annually; renewal requires re-verifying the renewer's employment via the Proxi.id partner, and the original applicant must be the one to renew (ownership transferable via support ticket). Multiple self-managed instances can run on the same license key. Community-level support only (GitLab Community Discord); paid support no longer offered as an add-on on this license. 1,000+ institutions and ~3M people participate; the program spans 65 countries.
  - *Eligibility:* Institution must be: accredited by a local/state/provincial/federal/national authorized agency, primarily focused on teaching enrolled students, actively granting degrees/diplomas/certificates, and not-for-profit (for-profit institutions ineligible). Institutions enrolling students under 13 are ineligible for SaaS (self-managed still available). Applicant must be a FULL-TIME faculty or staff member applying with the institution's email domain; GitLab explicitly cannot issue licenses directly to students — students must find a faculty/staff sponsor or use the Free tier. GitLab, Inc. does not issue licenses to institutions in China (JiHu separate entity). Use restricted to instruction and non-commercial academic research — not running/administering the institution's own IT services.
  - *How to claim:* Faculty/staff apply through the GitLab Customer Portal at customers.gitlab.com/subscriptions/community_program/coupons/new?program=EDU (linked from about.gitlab.com/solutions/education/join/), verify via institutional email, receive redemption instructions; re-apply/verify annually for renewal.
  - *Expiry:* License valid 1 year, renewed annually; access lapses when employment/renewal verification fails; graduates lose access because licenses are issued to the institution.
  - *Gotchas:* NO student-issued licenses — individual students with .edu emails are NOT automatically enrolled; a faculty/staff sponsor is required. 'Apply with the institution's email domain' (institutional domain, not necessarily .edu). No credit card required. No storage allotment is stated. Students are funneled to https://about.gitlab.com/community/ (Free tier) instead. China excluded; for-profit colleges excluded.
  - *Source:* https://about.gitlab.com/solutions/education/join/
- **GitLab for Startups (free/reduced Ultimate program)** (high) — Two tiers by funding: SEED — Pre-Seed or Seed stage with up to $5M in external funding, new customers only: Year 1 = free GitLab Ultimate license (SaaS or self-managed) up to 20 seats; Year 2 = 50% discount on any tier, up to 20 users. EARLY — Series A or Series B with up to $20M in external funding, new customers only: Year 1 = 50% discount on any tier (up to 20 users); Year 2 = 25% discount (up to 20 users). Program spans two years; GitLab contacts teams before year one ends to discuss renewal. No company-age or headcount threshold is stated. Support is NOT included; self-managed recipients must enable usage and analytics features. Additional seats beyond 20: email startups@gitlab.com for an add-on quote.
  - *Eligibility:* Externally funded startups only — self-funded companies are excluded ("The program is currently available for externally funded companies only"). Must be a new GitLab customer. Seed: <= $5M external funding; Early: Series A/B, <= $20M. Applicants must provide a Crunchbase, Pitchbook profile, or VC/accelerator webpage proving current funding status; otherwise the application "will not be considered". New customers only.
  - *How to claim:* Submit the in-page application form on https://about.gitlab.com/solutions/startups/ (#form anchor; all fields required; may be asked for more info to verify eligibility). Questions: startups@gitlab.com. No credit card requirement stated.
  - *Expiry:* Two-year program (Year 1 entitlement, then Year 2 discount); after year two the company "will have the flexibility to choose from our offerings"; program "offered at the sole discretion of GitLab and is subject to change". Applications not processed during U.S. holidays.
  - *Gotchas:* The current official page does NOT list 50,000 compute minutes or any storage allowance for startups (the pricing page's line bundling "qualifying open source projects, educational institutions, and startups" under "free Ultimate licenses, along with 50K compute minutes/month" is ambiguous — confirm with GitLab whether SaaS compute minutes ship with the seed license). 20-seat cap. Paid support excluded. Seed stage gets the only genuinely free license; Early stage is a discount, not free.
  - *Source:* https://about.gitlab.com/solutions/startups/
- **GitLab for Open Source (free Ultimate features for qualifying projects)** (high) — Features of GitLab Ultimate free to qualifying open source projects, self-managed or SaaS, plus 50,000 compute minutes "calculated at a program-specific cost factor." Seat allocation uses a concurrent-seat model (max users enabled at once; every occupied seat counts, including the owner's, except Guest-permission members). No support included, and purchasing GitLab Credits for the Duo Agent Platform is not available on community subscriptions.
  - *Eligibility:* Every project in the namespace must be published under an OSI-approved open source license; the organization must not seek to make a profit (donations OK; selling services/enhancements not); group and source code must be publicly visible and available; personal namespaces ineligible (must be a group; only free-tier groups you own appear in the application dropdown).
  - *How to claim:* Steps: (1) verify qualification against the requirements list, (2) create a GitLab account (Free tier or Ultimate trial), (3) GitLab.com: fill out the Customers Portal form to confirm the group meets requirements — you then receive an email with a coupon code and provisioning instructions; self-managed: submit the form with project details and a clear justification; (4) enroll via the Apply link. Renew annually by resubmitting the form; renew up to 3 months early, start at least 1 month before expiration — failing to renew means the account is downgraded.
  - *Expiry:* Annual membership, renewed by re-applying; downgraded to Free tier if not renewed. No CC required.
  - *Gotchas:* Non-commercial requirement rules out monetized/mixed projects; Guest seats don't count toward the concurrent cap; self-managed applications are not processed during holidays; 50K minutes are billed internally at a "program-specific cost factor" rather than standard rates.
  - *Source:* https://about.gitlab.com/solutions/open-source/join/
- **30-day GitLab Ultimate free trial (SaaS and self-managed)** (high) — "Start your 30-day GitLab Ultimate trial" covering "nearly all features of our Ultimate tier" plus Duo Agent Platform access. 30 days for both GitLab.com (hosted) and self-managed. GitLab.com trial limits: 400 compute minutes/month, 24 Duo Agent Platform credits/user, one project token only (group tokens excluded), no external-API features. Self-managed trial: requires GitLab 18.0+ and an internet connection, support excluded at any level, Duo Agent Platform (24 credits/user) starts with GitLab 18.9. A credit/debit card is NOT required for customers who do not use GitLab.com CI/CD (BYO runners or shared runners disabled); enabling shared runners triggers a $1 authorization (no charge transfers). New and existing users can start; at trial end you stay on the Free tier or upgrade, and "You will lose access to GitLab Duo Agent Platform and any remaining credits from the trial period." Included 12/24 Duo credits on paid tiers are a limited-time promotional offer.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone, including students and startup founders; no CC required unless shared runners are used; one trial flow via gitlab.com/-/trials/new or the free-trial page; no per-account trial-count cap or fixed expiry date is stated on the page.
  - *How to claim:* Go to https://about.gitlab.com/free-trial/ (or gitlab.com/-/trial_registrations/new), choose hosted or self-managed, sign up / log in.
  - *Expiry:* 30 days per trial; Duo Agent Platform access and remaining trial credits are forfeited at trial end.
  - *Gotchas:* Trial-vs-free: the 30-day Ultimate trial is separate from the permanent Free tier; post-trial you fall back to Free (with its 400-min/5-user caps) unless you pay. The marketing JSON-LD schemas on the /solutions/ pages still advertise a "60-day free trial" — the dedicated official trial page says 30 days; treat 30 days as current. Shared-runner use triggers a $1 card hold.
  - *Source:* https://about.gitlab.com/free-trial/
- **2025–26 change:** Restructured/changed in 2025-2026 (per official pages read 2026-10-22): (1) Trial duration — the dedicated free-trial page states 30-day GitLab Ultimate trials for both hosted and self-managed, while JSON-LD schema metadata on the marketing /solutions/ pages still says "60-day free trial"; the 30-day figure is the current authoritative one. (2) GitLab for Startups is now a two-tier (Seed <=$5M / Early Series A-B <=$20M) structure capping benefits at 20 seats, with Seed = free Ultimate year one + 50% year two and Early = 50%/25% discounts; self-funded startups are excluded and the current page no longer lists 50,000 compute minutes or a storage allowance for the startup program (the pricing page's "50K compute minutes/month" sentence bundles open source, education, and startups without clarifying which still get it — verify with startups@gitlab.com). (3) Education: seats are unlimited with 50,000 compute minutes/month included, but the license is institution-only via full-time faculty/staff — GitLab explicitly cannot issue licenses directly to students, so there is no standalone "GitLab for Students" free program; students are routed to the Free tier or a faculty sponsor. (4) Duo AI features: no free credits are bundled on the Free plan (paid GitLab Credits add-on, $1/credit); the 12/user/mo (Premium) and 24/user/mo (Ultimate) included-credits lines are flagged as a limited-time promotional offer, so those "free" AI credits can sunset. (5) Trial is Ultimate-only; no standalone Premium trial offer appears on the current trial page. No program was announced as fully sunset as of this check; open-source program renewals lapse to the Free tier if not resubmitted annually.

### JetBrains Student Pack

- **JetBrains Student Pack - free full IDE licenses for students** (high) — Free, non-commercial licenses for the paid IDEs while you stay enrolled; must be re-verified (renewed) annually with a reminder email. Products named in the Aug 2025 blog: IntelliJ IDEA, PyCharm, WebStorm, CLion, Rider, PhpStorm, DataGrip, RubyMine, RustRover, DataSpell, GoLand (11 IDEs). Also includes the JetBrains Academy plugin (100+ courses), IDE Features Trainer, and Code With Me. Non-commercial use only.
  - *Eligibility:* Student at an accredited educational institution (university/college; program longer than one year - bootcamps/short courses NOT eligible). Verify via: official university (.edu-like) email, ISIC/ITIC student ID, GitHub Student Developer Pack account, or uploaded official enrollment document (student ID/enrollment letter, <=4MB, not older than 6 months; China can use chsi.com.cn report; review up to 2 weeks). Minors need a parent/guardian email for consent. No credit card required. GitHub Student Developer Pack is a first-class application route (GitHub is a 'trusted partner').
  - *How to claim:* 1) Apply at jetbrains.com/estore/students (or the academy student-pack page) using a GitHub Student Developer Pack account, university email + ID, or official documents; 2) email verification link; 3) after approval, log in to a JetBrains Account, download any IDE in the list - license activates automatically; installation instructions arrive by email.
  - *Expiry:* Valid as long as you remain a student; renewal annually (JetBrains reminds by email; resubmit proof of enrollment). The Sep 12, 2025 prize-draw deadline has passed.
  - *Gotchas:* Non-commercial only - no commercial work. Free educational licenses exclude short-term/bootcamp programs (official FAQ, updated 30 Sep 2026). Annual re-verification required or access lapses. The 11 IDEs are individual products; the All Products Pack-style full bundle is what the pack effectively grants across them. AI tools are NOT included free - see separate finding.
  - *Source:* https://www.jetbrains.com/academy/student-pack/;https://www.jetbrains.com/estore/students/;https://blog.jetbrains.com/education/2025/08/12/jetbrains-student-pack/
- **JetBrains Teacher Pack - free IDEs for teachers/educators** (high) — Free access to 10 JetBrains IDEs - 'the full versions of IntelliJ IDEA and PyCharm, plus GoLand, PhpStorm, and others' - plus course creation (JetBrains Academy) and 'classroom-wide AI control, deployed and managed from one place'. Licenses are valid one year with an automatic email reminder before expiry to renew/re-apply. No percentage discount tier is shown on the page; the pack is free, and institutional projects beyond classroom use go to commercial licensing.
  - *Eligibility:* Two tracks: (a) classroom track - representatives of accredited schools/colleges/universities, verified by 'a letter from school officials or a copy of your staff ID'; (b) individual track - teachers and IT staff at accredited institutions with programs longer than one year, verified by a university email address or valid ISIC card. No credit card required. Apply through the JetBrains shop e-form.
  - *How to claim:* Apply via the 'Apply' link on the Teacher Pack page (JetBrains shop e-form), pick classroom or individual track, submit verification document or ISIC/university email, license arrives in the JetBrains account after review.
  - *Expiry:* Licenses valid 1 year; automatic email reminder before expiration to renew or re-apply (renewable while still teaching).
  - *Gotchas:* Classroom use is the intended scope; internal/institutional commercial projects must use commercial licenses. 'Full versions' means paid-tier IDEs, not just Community features.
  - *Source:* https://www.jetbrains.com/academy/teacher-pack/
- **JetBrains for Startups - 50% Startup Discount Plan** (high) — 50% off JetBrains developer, team and learning tools plus AI, for up to 10 IDE subscriptions, 10 paid team/learning tool subscriptions, and 10 JetBrains AI subscriptions (AI Pro/AI Ultimate, which include Junie) over a five-year window. Plugins: unlimited different plugins, up to 10 licenses each. TeamCity capped at 10 build agents; YouTrack at 250 users/subscription. Licenses can be spread across the 5 years; 50% off applies automatically at checkout; cannot be stacked with other discounts; no team/floating licenses; personal (non-commercial) licenses excluded.
  - *Eligibility:* Company privately owned; established within the last 5 years (provable with a document, <=4MB); has a website or other discoverable online presence. No funding cap, revenue cap, or headcount cap stated (teams of 50+ are redirected to business sales). One application per organization; subsidiaries of an already-approved org are ineligible; discount granted only once. Review takes up to 48 hours.
  - *How to claim:* Apply at jetbrains.com/startups (form: jetbrains.com/shop/eform/startup): choose products, submit company info + founding-date proof; if approved you get a quote email and 50% off automatically applies at checkout within the caps for 5 years.
  - *Expiry:* Discount lapses 5 years after the approval confirmation or when the purchase caps (10+10+10 subscriptions) are exhausted, whichever first. Company can receive the discount only once.
  - *Gotchas:* This is a discount program, not free licenses - the older 'up to 12 free startup licenses' model is gone (see sunset notes). No free tier for direct startup applicants; the free-license route now only exists via the partner track: incubators/accelerators/VCs with >=10 portfolio startups get six months of free access to up to 10 licenses, then the standard 50% for the remaining ~4 years; a partner-network startup must use the partner's free period first and cannot hold a direct discount at the same time. Credit card not needed to apply.
  - *Source:* https://www.jetbrains.com/startups/;https://www.jetbrains.com/store/startups/
- **JetBrains AI Free access - Junie Lite + AI Free plan (AI tools)** (medium) — Free AI layer for individuals: 'Junie Lite' is a free model, 'always on, in every JetBrains account' - 'a free AI layer, no trial, no card', plus BYOK (bring your own API key, provider-rate pricing, zero markup, supports local models). The Aug 2025 student-pack post says students should 'Test-drive JetBrains AI tools during your trial, and then switch to the AI Free plan or upgrade anytime'. Paid tiers for reference: AI Pro $8.33/user/month (10 AI credits per 30 days + top-ups); AI Ultimate $25/user/month (35 AI credits per 30 days + top-ups, recommended for Junie).
  - *Eligibility:* Any JetBrains account gets Junie Lite free (no card, no trial). AI Free plan available to individual users, including students after their trial. Paid tiers need a subscription. No region limits stated.
  - *How to claim:* Sign in to any JetBrains account / open Junie in terminal or IDE -> free model is available immediately. For AI Assistant: click the AI icon in the IDE top-right, complete trial, then select the AI Free plan; upgrade to Pro/Ultimate via the JetBrains subscription purchase links.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry stated for the free tier; 'always on, in every JetBrains account'.
  - *Gotchas:* Free tier is model- and rate-limited ('AI usage billed separately' caveat on the page); heavy Junie agent use hits the free-model or credit limits, which is why Ultimate is 'recommended for Junie'. Naming is in flux: product page says 'Junie Lite', student blog says 'AI Free plan' - the free tier's exact credit numbers were not published on the pages fetched. CC NOT required for the free tier.
  - *Source:* https://junie.jetbrains.com/;https://blog.jetbrains.com/education/2025/08/12/jetbrains-student-pack/
- **Free IDEs for anyone - non-commercial use & unified IDEA free tier** (high) — Several JetBrains products are free outside the student program: RustRover is listed on the products page with download only (no Buy CTA); Datalore is 'Get it for free'; YouTrack has a free tier plus paid; dotPeek is a free .NET decompiler; Gateway, Hub and Toolbox are free downloads. Since the July 2025 unified distribution (from 2025.3), IntelliJ IDEA ships as ONE installer: all Ultimate features need a subscription, but without one 'the IDE will remain fully functional, free to use for both commercial and non-commercial projects, and will include more features than the current Community Edition ever had'.
  - *Eligibility:* Nobody in particular - free for anyone: RustRover, Datalore, YouTrack (free tier), dotPeek, Gateway, Hub, Toolbox; IDEA's free feature set is usable even for commercial projects. Community Edition as a separate product no longer exists.
  - *How to claim:* Download directly from jetbrains.com/products (or the per-product pages); for IDEA, install the single unified IDE and use the free feature set without any license.
  - *Expiry:* No expiry; ongoing. (Per-product 'free for non-commercial use' tiers for WebStorm/GoLand-class IDEs are commonly cited but were not re-verified on today's pages - unconfirmed here.)
  - *Gotchas:* Unified IDEA still gates the full Ultimate feature set behind a subscription; when a paid IDEA subscription lapses, the IDE degrades to the free feature set instead of locking out; a perpetual fallback license (last major version from your uninterrupted-subscription year) still applies. Don't confuse free personal use of these IDEs with the student pack, which grants full paid IDEs to students.
  - *Source:* https://www.jetbrains.com/products/;https://blog.jetbrains.com/idea/2025/07/intellij-idea-unified-distribution-plan/
- **30-day free trials for paid JetBrains products** (medium) — All paid products (IntelliJ IDEA Ultimate features, WebStorm, CLion, DataGrip, Rider, TeamCity, etc.) offer a 30-day free trial, and the student-pack post explicitly points students to 'test-drive JetBrains AI tools during your trial' before moving to the AI Free plan.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone with a JetBrains account; no eligibility beyond creating an account.
  - *How to claim:* Download a paid product from its product page, start the IDE, choose the 30-day trial when prompted at license selection.
  - *Expiry:* 30 days per product (longer combined window when products are downloaded together).
  - *Gotchas:* Trial is not free-forever; CC not required for the trial itself. Do not mistake trials for the free student/teacher licenses.
  - *Source:* https://www.jetbrains.com/products/
- **40% graduation discount for 2 years (unverified today)** (low) ⚠ low confidence: verify before claiming — The task's known-baseline says '40% graduation discount for 2 years' - graduated students can keep cheap licenses for their first two years after finishing studies. This could NOT be re-verified today: jetbrains.com/estore pages are behind a Cloudflare check to this fetcher, and /shop/eform/students, /estore/graduates, /shop/eform/graduates, /for-startups all returned 404/403. No official page read today contains the figure.
  - *Eligibility:* Per the old program: recent graduates who held a student license; claimed through the students e-store with proof of graduation/completion.
  - *How to claim:* Unconfirmed today - check jetbrains.com/estore/students or the JetBrains sales FAQ directly for the live terms.
  - *Expiry:* Believed to apply for up to 2 years after graduation; unconfirmed.
  - *Gotchas:* May have been folded into the Student Pack / estore flow after the Aug 2025 relaunch; do not quote the 40%/2-years figures without re-checking the official page.
  - *Source:* https://www.jetbrains.com/estore/students/(pagenotfullyrenderedthissession)
- **2025–26 change:** 2025-2026 changes: (1) Aug 12, 2025 - JetBrains rebranded/relaunched the student program as the "JetBrains Student Pack" (blog.jetbrains.com post); license duration wording is now "completely free and remain valid as long as you're a student, but must be renewed annually" (replaces the old "1-year, renewable" framing). Promo: verify by Sep 12, 2025 to enter a prize draw (MacBook Pro, iPad Pro, reMarkable Paper Pro) - now expired. (2) Bootcamp student-coupon program is being retired: official sales FAQ (updated 30 Sep 2026) states "no new coupons are being issued after October 1, 2026"; short-term/bootcamp students were never eligible for free educational licenses (only long-term programs >1 year qualify). (3) Jul 18, 2025 - IntelliJ IDEA moved to a unified distribution starting with 2025.3: the separate Community Edition installer is gone; the single IDE keeps more free features than old Community for everyone, and an expired subscription degrades gracefully to the free feature set instead of locking out. Perpetual fallback license (last major version after a year of uninterrupted subscription) still applies. (4) The startup offer is now a 50% discount plan (caps over 5 years), not the older "up to 12 free licenses for 1 year" program that ran for years - free startup licenses survive only in the incubator/accelerator/VC partner track (6 months, up to 10 licenses). The 40% / 2-year graduation discount listed in the task's "known so far" could not be re-verified today: jetbrains.com student/estore pages are Cloudflare-gated to this fetcher and /shop/eform/students, /estore/graduates, /for-startups, /startup all 404'd; treat it as likely still real but unconfirmed (no official page read today).

### Linear

- **Free plan (always-free tier)** (high) — $0 forever. Unlimited members, up to 2 teams, 250 active issues (unlimited archived issues), 10 MB file uploads per attachment, Google SSO, core issue tracking plus Agent platform and Linear Agent. This is the de-facto 'trial' for everyone — Linear runs no separate paid-plan trial; sign-up itself is free.
  - *Eligibility:* Anyone, worldwide, no account-age, funding or headcount limits. Sign up with email, Google or GitHub — credit card NOT required. No .edu needed.
  - *How to claim:* 1. Go to linear.app and sign up (free). 2. Create workspace; you get 2 teams / 250 issues immediately. 3. Add unlimited teammates for free. No application, no approval.
  - *Expiry:* None published; free-plan limits (unlimited members, 2 teams, 250 issues, 10MB uploads) unchanged across every archived pricing page 2024-10-2026.
  - *Gotchas:* 250 active-issue cap (new issue creation blocks at cap, archived issues exempt); only 2 teams; 10MB upload cap; AI-credit-dependent features (Coding Sessions, Loops, agent automations) are metered separately on paid tiers — free tier has no paid AI features. Sales tax now applies in specific US states + DC (as of Jan 2026) on paid plans, not free.
  - *Source:* https://linear.app/pricing
- **Startup program — up to 6 months free Business plan (partner referral only)** (high) — Startups affiliated with an official Linear partner (VC, accelerator or startup community; 500+ partners incl. a16z, Sequoia, Index, Techstars, Antler, Bessemer, 500 Global) get up to 6 months of free access to the Business plan (normally $16/user/mo annual). At redemption the account automatically renews at the standard rate unless you switch plans or cancel.
  - *Eligibility:* Must be affiliated with a partner (dedicated partner link/code). Hard cap: fewer than 50 employees AND non-paying Linear user (new paid customers only). No funding cap, company-age cap or revenue cap published. Bootstrapped/unaffiliated founders: NOT eligible — the page explicitly says only partner-affiliated companies can participate; >50 employees: contact sales instead.
  - *How to claim:* 1. Find your investor/accelerator/community in the partner list on linear.app/startups (ask them to join if missing). 2. Ask the partner for their dedicated redemption link. 3. As workspace admin, follow the link or enter the partner code to redeem; offer applies to a new workspace. No codes to manage on the startup side.
  - *Expiry:* Program open-ended (no sunset date published); each grant lasts up to 6 months, then auto-renews at standard Business rate unless cancelled/switched. Older third-party listings mentioning 'Standard and Plus plans' are stale (2024 plan names) — current grant covers Basic or Business.
  - *Gotchas:* Referral-only: no self-service application and no bootstrapped path. 50-employee cap and 'non-paying users only' are the effective gates. Duration not precisely published per grant ('up to 6 months'). AI-credit-gated features (agent automations, Loops, Code Intelligence) may incur extra AI-credit charges on top of the free seats. After the free window, workspace drops to paying Business rate for all users, not just admins.
  - *Source:* https://linear.app/startups
- **Education program — students: 1 year free (100% off Basic or Business)** (high) — Full-time students at colleges/universities get 100% discount (free) on Basic ($10/user/mo annual) or Business ($16/user/mo annual) for ONE YEAR, for student-led programs, clubs and educational activities (non-commercial). No credit card required to claim (a $0 charge at checkout is mentioned by aggregators).
  - *Eligibility:* .edu email required to apply. Must be full-time; high schools, for-profit and online-only institutions excluded. Proof of full-time enrollment required — student IDs are NOT accepted (enrollment letter / proof of enrolled courses). Optionally attach proof of institutional accreditation to speed approval. Not for commercial/business use. No region limits published (non-.edu academic domains possible but undocumented — ask support).
  - *How to claim:* 1. Create a Linear workspace (name it — needed for the application). 2. Email support@linear.app FROM your .edu address, including your workspace name and the email on the Linear account. 3. Attach proof of full-time enrollment (not a student ID) and optionally accreditation proof. 4. Wait for manual approval (no self-service portal); discount is then applied to Basic or Business.
  - *Expiry:* Free period = 1 year per grant. Renewal/re-verification rules NOT published — unclear if renewable; check the approval email.
  - *Gotchas:* Applies to Basic/Business only, NOT Enterprise. Covers student/educational use only — commercial side projects out of scope. Approval is at Linear's sole discretion and can change terms. If the workspace exceeds free-tier caps on any paid downgrade/cancellation, new issue creation blocks. Third-party value quotes ($120/yr Basic, $192/yr Business per seat at 2026 prices) reflect the post-Nov-2025 price hike.
  - *Source:* https://linear.app/docs/billing-and-plans
- **Education program — university staff: 75% off Basic/Business (ongoing)** (high) — Staff and employees at qualifying academic institutions (colleges and higher-level institutions) get a 75% discount on Basic and Business paid plans. Discount is ongoing per approval (no published end date, unlike the 1-year student grant).
  - *Eligibility:* .edu/school email + proof of employment at an eligible college or university. Same institutional exclusions as the student track (no high schools, for-profit or online-only institutions). At Linear's sole discretion.
  - *How to claim:* Email support@linear.app from your institutional .edu address with workspace name, account email and proof of employment; optionally attach proof of your institution's accreditation. Discount applied on approval.
  - *Expiry:* No sunset published; discount runs with the subscription. Enterprise plan excluded (75% applies to Basic and Business only).
  - *Gotchas:* You still pay 25% of seat price (2026: $2.50/user/mo Basic, $4/user/mo Business equivalent). Excluded from discount: Enterprise tier and any AI-credit add-ons (metered separately).
  - *Source:* https://linear.app/docs/billing-and-plans
- **Non-profit program: 75% off Basic/Business** (high) — Registered non-profits get 75% off Basic and Business plans (not Enterprise). Apply via support@linear.app with proof of non-profit status; eligibility determined at Linear's sole discretion.
  - *Eligibility:* Standard 501(c)-style registered non-profit status proof. Explicitly EXCLUDED: legislative/political bodies, churches and religious groups, schools and colleges, government offices, opinion-influence groups, hospitals/health-insurance entities, and private foundations.
  - *How to claim:* Email support@linear.app with proof of non-profit status (registration docs); discount applied on approval.
  - *Expiry:* No sunset published; runs with the subscription. 75% covers Basic/Business only.
  - *Gotchas:* Discretionary approval; a long list of org types is excluded even if formally non-profit; AI credits remain a separate metered cost.
  - *Source:* https://linear.app/docs/billing-and-plans
- **No standalone free trial / trial credits for paid plans** (high) — Linear offers no time-boxed trial of Basic/Business and no trial credits anywhere in 2025-2026. The free plan is the only always-free access; paid access comes via the partner-startup grant or the education/non-profit discounts above.
  - *Eligibility:* N/A — absence confirmed on pricing page, docs and startup page as of 2026-10-22.
  - *How to claim:* Not claimable.
  - *Expiry:* N/A
  - *Gotchas:* Don't confuse partner startup grants (up to 6 months free Business) with a general trial — they require partner affiliation. Anyone else paying gets Basic $10/user/mo annual (no published monthly-billing price) or Business $16/user/mo annual.
  - *Source:* https://linear.app/pricing

### Microsoft

- **Azure for Students: $100 credit + 12-month free services + always-free services (no credit card)** (high) — As read 2026-10-22: $100 in Azure credit valid for 12 months (new Azure customers only); free monthly amounts of 20+ popular services for 12 months (e.g., 750 hrs each of B1s/B2pts v2/B2ats v2 burstable VMs; Container Registry 1 Standard tier with 100 GB + 10 webhooks; Azure SQL 100,000 vCore-sec serverless/mo with 32 GB; PostgreSQL/MySQL 750 hrs Flexible B1MS with 32 GB data + 32 GB backup; SQL Managed Instance 720 vCore-hrs of 4/8-vCore GP with 64 GB data + 64 GB backup); plus 65+ always-free services (DevOps: 5 users with unlimited private Git repos; Cosmos DB 1,000 RU/s + 25 GB; Functions 1M requests/mo; App Service up to 10 web/API apps w/ 1 GB storage; Static Web Apps 100 GB bandwidth; AKS cluster mgmt free; IoT Hub 8,000 msgs/day). Renew annually while a student. One account per person. After credit: email offers pay-as-you-go or disable.
  - *Eligibility:* Full-time university students only (page asterisks full-time requirement); sign up with school email to verify student status; .edu or equivalent institutional email; not a trial — no credit card required. Region-limited services apply (always-free/12-month SKUs differ by Azure region); available in most Azure regions.
  - *How to claim:* 1) Go to azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students. 2) Sign up with a Microsoft account + school email. 3) Student status verified automatically from email (no CC). 4) Claim $100 credit and apply; browse always-free/12-month services in portal. 5) Renew yearly while enrolled.
  - *Expiry:* Credit: 12 months from account creation; 12-month services: 12 months (new customers); always-free services: indefinite. Annual renewal while a student re-extends the free amounts. No published sunset date.
  - *Gotchas:* Credit card NOT required, but services beyond free amounts/credit bill you once credit is gone (pay-as-you-go). 'Always-free' has hard monthly caps (egress >100 GB/mo always-free is billed; data egress costs apply). Student plan is for learning/non-commercial; commercial workloads are ineligible. One account per person; re-verification may be requested.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
- **GitHub Student Developer Pack: free GitHub Pro, free Copilot, 80+ partner dev-tool offers (JetBrains, CI/CD)** (high) — As read 2026-10-22 (pack page footer '© 2026'): free GitHub Pro account while a student (unlimited private repos; 3,000 Actions minutes/mo; 180 Codespaces hours/mo; 2 GB Packages + 20 GB Codespaces storage), free GitHub Copilot Student tier ('unlimited code completions, an allowance of GitHub AI Credits, plus limited chat/agent usage'), free Microsoft 365 offer in the pack ('with AI built in, 1 TB cloud storage, advanced security'), Microsoft Azure in the pack: '$100 in Azure credit + 25+ Azure services' (students 18+; a separate 13–17 offer: free App Service, Functions, Notification Hubs, MySQL, App Insights, Azure DevOps). Partner offers incl. JetBrains 'free subscription for students, renewed annually', Heroku $13/mo credit for 24 months, 1Password free 2 years, Datadog Pro (10 servers) 2 years, MongoDB $50 Atlas credit + $150 certification, GitKraken & GitLens free 6 months then up to 80% off while a student, Travis CI private builds while a student, Codecov free public+private, BrowserStack 1 year, Sentry (50K errors) 1 yr renewable, Requestly Pro ($270 value) 1 yr. 83 partner entries total (page says '80+' style count).
  - *Eligibility:* Students aged 13+ enrolled in a degree- or diploma-granting course of study; school-issued email address and/or official, dated proof of enrollment. .edu preferred but international equivalents accepted via verification. One pack per student. Free; no credit card for GitHub itself (some partner trials may ask for a card on their side).
  - *How to claim:* 1) github.com/education/students (or education.github.com/pack). 2) 'Get verified': sign in, verify school email or upload enrollment proof. 3) Claim Student Developer Pack; individual partner offers are accepted one by one in the pack page. Renewal: re-verify yearly while enrolled.
  - *Expiry:* Pack entitlement lasts while student status is verified; renews annually. Individual partner offers have their own terms (1 week–24 months; e.g., Heroku $13/mo x 24 mo). 'Always' items: GitHub Pro, Copilot Student, Codespaces pro tier.
  - *Gotchas:* GitHub Actions minutes/Codespaces hours are tied to the free Pro student tier, not a one-time grant. Copilot 'limited chat/agent usage' caps apply. Partner lineup churns (e.g., Gitpod/Replit/DigitalOcean no longer listed in 2026; JetBrains still listed but now as 'free subscription, renewed annually'). Microsoft 365 offer in the pack is the discounted/free M365 deal, not a new entitlement.

Desktop Office apps install on up to 5 PCs/Macs + up to 5 tablets/phones per user; includes web apps, OneDrive for Education, Teams for Education. Renew yearly while enrolled.

You must convert to pay-as-you-go to keep running after credit expiry. The AI free allowances are rebranded under Microsoft Foundry ('Foundry Tools'/'Control Plane') in 2025-2026 — same services, new names. One account per person; no stacking with the Azure Free account's $200.

The $25/75-day extension that older guides mention is no longer advertised on the current page. Spending protection applies only while on the free account; convert to PAYG and all usage is billed.

Credits grow via automatic milestones: $5,000 after business verification (registered legal entity); $25,000 after 5+ active Azure workloads sustained ~60 days (one-time 2-year credit-use extension granted at this tier); $50,000 at 7+ workloads; $150,000 at 10+ workloads AND ~$3,000/month sustained usage. You can skip tiers; a 10-character investor referral code (from a Microsoft for Startups Investor Network VC/accelerator/partner university) unlocks higher tiers faster, a dedicated Microsoft point of contact, and extra credits. Includes Azure AI/Foundry model access, Marketplace/co-sell benefits, expert guidance.
  - *Eligibility:* Early-stage B2B software startups: pre-seed through Series B (no Series C funding or later), privately held for-profit, building a software product you own, HQ in a country where Azure is available; NOT more than $350,000 lifetime free Azure credits; no educational institutions/government/consultancies; no crypto mining. Apply with a personal consumer email (school/work emails rejected). Decisions typically within 3 business days.

Higher tiers require sustained ~60-day workload usage (each workload must stay above $1 spend for all 60 days; the clock starts once the required number of workloads each have >=$1 spend). No fixed sunset date published.
  - *Gotchas:* A credit cap, not cash: money is only 'free' while inside the ceiling, and exceeding it moves you to pay-as-you-go. Payment info/card is required at activation (won't be charged unless you exceed credits and stay on PAYG). Milestone credits are conditional on real sustained usage, not activation — many startups stop at $5K. School/edu emails are explicitly rejected for signup (contrast with the student program). The $150K figures on the still-live Founders Hub signup page and the $10K/$350K caps reflect different (legacy vs current) rules — the Learn program docs are authoritative. Pegasus (invitation-only) program continues separately.
  - *Source:* https://learn.microsoft.com/en-us/startups/microsoft-for-startups/getting-started-mfs

This is the self-serve track of the July 2025 MFS restructure (replacing the old $150K/$10K Founders tiers for un-backed startups), which Microsoft further streamlined in 2026 into the milestone model ($200 start -> $5K verified -> $25K/$50K/$150K milestones).

  - *Gotchas:* No dedicated support on the self-serve track (self-service only) — the dedicated-Microsoft-POC/support path requires an Investor Network referral. Card required (unlike the student tier). The $4,000 is gated on actually completing business verification within the window. Note the 2026 milestone docs show some tiers now framed as $200/$5,000 gates, so the $1K/$4K structure coexists with the milestone model — check which offer is presented at signup.
  - *Source:* https://azure.microsoft.com/en-us/free/students
